Previously on MTNFA:

Agatha Harkness...Your presence is requested

Agatha took a calculated risk, but man is she bad at math... Or The Ancient Ones are determined to ruin Agatha and Rio's lives.

    Chapter 1

    How Long's It Been, Agatha?

    Chapter Summary The coven waits for Agatha to return from her summons.

    Chapter Notes Welcome, darlings! It's the last full moon of the year. (Officially full at 3:02 am CT) Let's get on with the show, shall we? 😈 See the end of the chapter for more notes Tick...tock...tick...tock The sound of a clock filled the house. It counted down the seconds as gentle snores drifted throughout the rooms. Nicholas Scratch crawled out of bed and grabbed his stuffed version of Señor Scratchy.

    The growing child padded over to his mama's room with gentle pats on the hardwood floor. Nicholas took a deep breath, ignoring the chilly draft in the darkened hallway. He hugged his rabbit tight before pushing the door open.

    Pale moonlight bathed the room in a soft glow. Dust motes drifted lazily in the silence, visible in the moonbeam. The queen bed was still made. Not a thing was out of place. Not a thing had been touched.

    Nicholas sighed heavily before pulling the door shut.

    It had been a long fourteen hours for Death. So many souls. So much pleading. She didn't hate her job, she just wished they didn't blame her. Death didn't kill them. Didn't interfere. She'd done that once and never again. The cosmic entity reasoned that Tommy and Billy didn't count because Agatha had been the one to interfere there.

    Rio slipped into Agatha's house, silent as a grave. Her hazel eyes landed on the perfectly smooth comforter and plump pillows. The scent of lavender had begun to fade without her wife. She was starting to wonder if Agatha was ever coming back now.

    Death left the room in search of the coven.

    Jeff and Rebecca moved about the kitchen with soft mumbles to one another. The sound of plates and silverware clinking against each other grated on Death's nerves as Rebecca moved to set the table. It was saying something, how easily they moved in the space. It was to be expected given the circumstances, but it still weighed heavily on the Green Witch. A handful of coven members sat at the dining room table with an assortment of morning beverages. The adults were dressed for the most part. Wanda watched over Nicky who was still in his pajamas; a book set before them. She was explaining idioms to the boy. He still had much to learn about his new home.

    The head of the table remained vacant.

    Wanda looked up from the book and shook her head with a sad smile. "Still nothing." She shut the book and pushed it away from her and Nicky. "Are you sure you can't go up there and get her?"

    Death fought a snarl. As if she hadn't tried everything to re-enter the Ancient Ones' temple to free her wife from whatever punishment they'd imposed on her. It was killing Death to not know where her wife was. To have no way of communicating with the goddess. Rio had, from the moment the portal closed, felt Agatha's punishment be sentenced. Her heart had seized for ten agonizing seconds before the next beat. Since then, the thudding in her chest had slowed to less than fifty beats every twenty-four hours. Not exactly a good sign, but it meant Agatha was alive in some sense. It was the only thing keeping her from tearing the Universe apart.

    Rio glowered. "Can't go up there unless they want you to," she reminded the witch for the hundredth time. Hazel eyes took attendance. "Where's Billy?"

    "Room," Alice told her. She straightened out her uniform, badge glinting in the light. "He's playing Evanescence, so I'd give him some space."

    Rio turned away from the table to find the teen. Based on the extra empty chair, Tommy was with him too. She stepped down into the basement where the two had taken up residence. Angsty punk rock that could only belong to Billy greeted her auricular orifices. She couldn't help but chuckle. He moped a lot like Agatha did. Hiding in dark places and trying to make themselves the bad guys. It was something she was going to try harder to keep the young Maximoff-Kaplan-Harkness-Vidal from doing if she could help it. Didn't want any of the children in the coven to suffer the same fate Agatha had been given. The teen didn't look up as she reached the bottom step. "I don't want to go up there," Billy muttered, his back to her. He was getting better about noticing her presence. She felt a prickle of pride for the teen.

    Death sat on the last step and stared into the room. Two cots were shoved up against the far sides of the room. It was cramped, more so than usual. Billy had taken an old radio and set it up to connect with his phone. Tommy sat on his cot, facing his brother as he leaned forward on his knees. "Bro's bein' stubborn," the speedster informed. "You got this Ma Cubed?" Rio nodded. "Jeff made breakfast. Can you make sure Nicky eats?"

    He fired off double finger-guns at her before racing up the stairs. She barely felt him as he passed her. Soon, he'd be faster than the speed of light. That would make for an interesting day.

    "You don't have to baby me," Billy grumbled. He rolled over and reached for the potted bonsai tree resting on a shelf. Billy held Bobby in his lap, a finger toying with a leaf. The cosmic entity sat in silence. Much like Agatha, he'd tell her what was on his mind in time. She could wait. Some woman wailed on the speaker. A haunting, phantom-like sound that only reminded Death of her wife. The way her soul had cried out before disappearing in the Ancient Ones' summon. Death seethed. Rio knew her patience with the panel was waning quickly. Another day longer, and she was going to start purposely killing off the forests. "What if I'm the reason Agatha hasn't come back?"

    Rio tilted her head, humming. It was a very slim possibility, but she couldn't rule it out entirely. "As the Demiurge, you do shape reality around you. I doubt that's the case, though." Billy put Bobby down by his feet and pressed the heels of his palms to his eyes. "I was mad, but I didn't want her to disappear like that."

    "You didn't make Agatha disappear," she told him. "Break her heart, maybe, but you didn't kill her or anything." Rio leaned back, elbows resting on the steps digging into her back. "She was summoned for breaking a rule. Not because you were mad at her."

    "But what does that even mean? Who are they?" He removed his hands from his eyes to stare at her. He looked so much more like a child without the dark make-up. Death was starting to understand why Agatha had been swayed by him in the first place. There was the smallest of similarities between Billy and Nicky.

    Rio shrugged. If they wouldn't give her wife back, maybe they'd yank her up for divulging sensitive information. "They're five old biddies that showed up a little after my siblings and I were...born. They carry out the will of the Universe, which is just my siblings' will." Billy stared at her, brows furrowing. "You have siblings?"

    The cosmic entity chuckled. At least he hadn't lost himself to his depression. "A brother and a sister," she told him. "But we don't see each other much. They stay in their realms." "Okay...So, these Ancient Ones. What do they want with you and Agatha?" "Control, mostly. They exist to keep me from stepping out of line." She didn't add that they'd failed when she met Agatha. Balance and Order took a back seat the second Death laid eyes on her promised wife. The last time she tipped the scales in her favor was for Nicky. It had been a one-time thing. She would never do it again. (Not unless Agatha asked her to.) "But you're the flow of Life and Death. Why are they needed? Why do they get to tell you what to do?"

    Rio sighed. The women had been instated for a specific reason. One she wasn't keen on divulging just yet. "That's just how things are, Billy. Not much I can do unless the Universe votes to disband them."

    "Does Agatha know?"

    She huffed out a laugh. The boy loved his mother. She had no doubt. He found a way to work her in at every chance. "She doesn't. She'd try to fight them if she knew." "Maybe she's fighting the Ancient Ones right now," he said, voice full of naive hope. The teen was still so young. He would never be able to handle the truth of the situation. How Agatha being gone for so long was actually a bad sign. But like she protected Nicky, she'd protect Tommy and Billy. Agatha would want her to.

    Rio protected that spark of hope as if it were a budding rose. "Maybe." She stood and waved him over. "You need to eat. Afterwards, I'll show you how to properly plant a seed. Agatha would be pissed if she found out you weren't continuing your lessons." Billy shuffled after her, Bobby in his hands. "So...you can hear us through plants, right?" "Mostly vascular ones. Occasionally, moss will speak to me." She stepped into the sunlight with a low whistle. The place smelled heavily of eggs, bacon, and coffee. "You almost missed out," she told the boy. "Better get a plate before it's all gone."

    He stopped her gently, careful not to push his luck. "I was just wondering...Can Agatha hear us through anything?"

    "She's the moon," Rio reminded. "Try talking to her through a moonbeam."

    Billy lay in his cot, eyes trained on the center of the room. He could barely make out the names of his fallen coven members. The Road was still down there. Waiting. Based on the even breathing of his twin, he was the only one awake. Tommy had crashed minutes after hitting the pillow. He'd run errands around town for Jen. Pretty soon, he'd need new pants. The friction tears were starting to get noticeable.

    Billy sat up before quietly getting off of his cot. He waited to see if his twin would wake up before scaling the stairs. The rest of the house was dark as his parents snored away on the pull-out couch. The teen nearly shouted in fright as he saw a small figure move in front of a curtain.

    "Nicky...," he whispered. Billy took a cautious step towards the den.

    His brother turned and waved him over. "I couldn't sleep," Nicky told him. Billy slunk over, careful not to wake his parents. "Yeah, but you can't hide in the dark like that," he said. The pair sat on the floor, staring out the glass patio doors. The moon was high in the sky, light cascading down. The backyard bathed in the light as if it were actually the sun. The celestial body made the teen frown.

    "I miss Mama," his younger brother told him. "I want her to come back home already." The teen hugged the boy to him with a sniffle. "I miss Agatha, too."

    Nicky pulled back slightly to look up at him. "Why do you call her that? She's our mama." Billy thought for a moment. He knew she was his mother in a spiritual sense. Had seen the way she caved to most of his whims. Had enjoyed being the center of her attention when she was a ghost. But he rarely referred to her as such. Usually, he called her 'mom' when he was joking around with her or Teddy. But after watching her be practically kidnapped, she wasn't just some cranky witch to him anymore. At the same time, they'd had that argument that left him feeling less than filial.

    "Why do you call Jen and Alice your 'sister'?" he said instead. He'd need more time to think about the Agatha of it all.

    Nicky shrugged. "They're our sisters in the coven. But Mama is still our mama. She always looks out for us, even when we get on her nerves." He held up his rabbit before passing it over to the teen. "I think you need this more than I do right now."

    Billy laughed softly as he took the plushie. "Why me?"

    Green eyes met with his. Suddenly, the seven-year-old didn't look seven. He seemed older beyond his years. "Because you don't have as many good memories of her as I do. I can remember her singing me to sleep and playing in the fields around the towns. All you've known is the loudness of this world. Scratchy can help cheer you up. Mama made him to help when things get rough."

    Billy looked down at the stuffed rabbit. He didn't sense any magic, but the stitching looked worn. There was a certain warmness that emanated from fabric. It felt...loved. "When did she have time to make you a plushie?"

    Nicky yawned before resting against his brother's side. "Mother said she stitched it up for me before I was born, but didn't get to give it to me. After you gave me a body, Mama had Mother get it so I could finally have it."

    The pair sat in the quiet. Eventually, Nicky fell asleep. His body weighed almost nothing as the child pressed into his side. Billy thought of Agatha and what she would do in the situation. Carefully, he focused on bringing his blanket up from the basement. He nearly cheered as it appeared without anything being destroyed in the process. Billy took the blanket and wrapped it around him and Nicky.

    "Come home, Agatha," he mumbled to the moonlight. "I've got levitation down. Time for my next lesson." Billy yawned before falling asleep against the glass of the door.

    Billy jerked awake as a hand shook him. Outside, the sun had yet to fully rise. The sky was in the process of changing from violet to orange. Birds chirped cheerily. Agatha would have hated it.

    Wanda smiled down at him; a robe wrapped around her. "Take Nicky and go lay in my bed," she whispered to him. "I'll get breakfast made."

    The teen nodded sleepily. He cracked his neck before scooping his brother up. Billy grunted as he tried to figure out the best way to carry a small child. Agatha and Rio make it look so easy, he told himself.

    There was a crash in the living room.

    Wanda had her magic at the ready Billy followed her, Nicky held to his chest. The rest of the house had been woken up by the noise. Tommy sped up from the basement, a bat in his hands. Even Rio had appeared. She had her dagger at the ready with a scowl. "Gah," a lump of fabric gasped. It shifted and rolled until auburn hair appeared. A hush fell over the house right as Rio's dagger clattered to the floor. Agatha turned over until she was sitting on the floor and looking at the faces around her. Her eyes were wide as she scanned the room. The goddess tapped the floor before moving to stand. She grunted as she swayed. Her legs immediately gave out on her. Rio was at her side, a tear crashing to the floor. "You're back," she breathed. Agatha licked her lips. "I'm..." Her voice was hoarse. She pressed a hand to her throat as if she were in shock. "I'm home," she told her wife. Agatha raised a hand before gently, ever so gently, wiping the tear away. She let out a breathy laugh before pulling Death into a kiss. Billy swallowed thickly. His mother was home. Had she heard him after all? The timing was too coincidental for his tastes. But he wasn't going to complain now that she was under the same roof as them. He cleared his throat as the kiss drug on for far longer than necessary. "Welcome back," he dared to tell her.

    The Divine Mother looked around the room again, brows furrowing. Her divine robes were still tattered and bloodied from her fight with Dr. Strange. Other than that, she looked as healthy as she had the last time the coven had seen her. Agatha tried to stand, only to sway dangerously to the side. Rio caught her, a hand wrapping around the younger immortal's waist.

    "Jen, get her something to drink," Wanda whispered. "She seems disoriented." Billy set the sleeping Nicky down on a now-vacant couch. He heard Rio whisper heatedly, "What did they do to you?" His stomach sank at the venom in her voice. He hadn't heard her sound like that since Agatha's death.

    His divine mother held up a hand to cut Death off. "I'm fine," she croaked. "I'm tired." Jen returned with a glass of water and passed it to Rio. "We missed you," the witch admitted. "It wasn't the same without you, Harkness. Way too quiet and peaceful." Agatha jerked her head back. "Missed me?" She looked around the room, still in apparent disbelief. "How long was I d-...How long was I gone?"

    The coven shuffled and fidgeted.

    "A month," Billy told her. "Give or take." He shifted his weight onto the opposite foot. "We've been trying to reach you, but nothing seemed to be working."

    His other mother swallowed thickly as she fixed her hair. He almost missed the way her face hardened. Was she still mad at him?

    "Yes, well, I was busy." She cleared her throat and rose from the floor. Agatha finally managed to stand up successfully. Although, she still needed Rio's help to hold steady. "Why are you all in my house?"

    "We live here," Alice laughed. "Or had you forgotten?"

    The goddess pursed her lips, eyes drifting away for a moment. "It's a mess," she finally said. "Where's Nicholas?"

    The entire house gave her a look. She had never referred to her child as 'Nicholas.' Billy put a protective hand over his brother. "Why did you say that?"

    Agatha rolled her eyes. "That is his name, you guys. I should know. I named him." She tapped the closest couch before gripping it firmly. "Is he still sleeping? He's a heavy sleeper." Billy held up a hand to stop her. "How do we know you're really Agatha? Prove it!" The Divine Mother took immediate offense. "If you don't back away from my son, I'll move you myself," she growled. Her magic cracked like a bolt of lightning.

    Definitely Agatha. "I had to make sure. You're acting funny," he told her as he stepped away. "You spend eter-" The witch snapped her mouth shut, her throat bobbing. Agatha looked away from him to gaze down at her sleeping child. "Nicholas," she whispered. Her voice cracked with unshed tears.

    "Let him sleep," Rio told her. "I want to make sure they didn't hurt you." Death tugged at Agatha's arm.

    Billy shared a look with Tommy. Ma Squared looks crazy, bro, his twin thought. Billy nodded. "We'll make you breakfast," the young witch told his mother. "Anything specific?" Agatha looked at him, mouth parting slightly. Her eyes shifted over to the Kaplan's. "I don't know if I'm hungry. I don't..." She furrowed her brows, eyes dropping to the floor. "I'm fine. Eat without me."

    The teen looked away, irritation coiling within him. Was she still mad? He'd said terrible things, but she did too! Why was he the one being punished? "Fine. Be like that." He turned away from her to go back down into the basement.

    Agatha eyed the room warily. She swallowed thickly as they stopped watching William leave before turning back to her. These people were going to have questions. Rio was going to have questions. They couldn't know. She didn't want them to know. The eyes boring into her were beginning to drive her mad. She didn't want to see them. She didn't want to be seen. Everything felt wrong.

    "Please get me when Nicholas wakes up." She tugged Rio to her.

    Her wife led her up the stairs, her hazel eyes full of concern. Agatha wanted to kiss them. To whisper that everything was fine. But she couldn't. There was an emotion bubbling to the surface that the others didn't need to see. Not if they were real. She clutched desperately at anything she could feel. Thankfully, that was Rio. The warm hand grounded her as they entered a bedroom.

    My bedroom, she reminded herself. It looked different from the way she remembered it. Maybe because it was clean. Maybe because she hadn't seen it in so long. Agatha patted the mattress before sitting down. She felt the coolness of the comforter. Traced the stitching and lace designs in silent awe.

    Rio was on her knees, hands clutching at Agatha's free hand. "Where did you go? What did they do to you?"

    Neither of them was stupid.

    Agatha knew Rio knew a harsh punishment had been doled out for the goddess. Harsh wasn't exactly how Agatha would have described it, though. Cruel, maybe. Torturous. It was certainly mind-altering. All the things that would make Death fly into a furious rage. She couldn't let that happen.

    The Ancient Ones knew, now, what they could do to break the Great Agatha Harkness. She wasn't keen on going back so soon. Agatha still refused to fully comply with their ham-fisted attempts to control her. But her 'time-outs' would only work for so long before their punishments fell on Nicholas again. That, she remembered clearly.

    "I don't want to talk about it," she breathed out. Agatha cupped the face gazing up at her. Traced the warm skin with her cool fingers in a trance. "I missed you." Rio pushed herself upwards to hug the goddess. "I missed you so much," Death choked out. "I couldn't feel you. I was so worried. I thought they'd taken you from me." Agatha closed her eyes as she sunk into the hug. She pressed her nose into her wife's hair and inhaled. Damp leaves, petrichor, and dirt. This was her Rio, all right. She ignored the cosmic entity's words. Didn't want to be reminded of her absence. She just wanted to be held. They did, stayed firmly hidden away deep within her soul. Another day, she'd tell Death what she endured.

    "You need to rest," Rio told her.

    Her fingers dug into Death's back. "No," she bit out. "No, I want to be up. I'm staying up." Her heart thudded in her chest as she began to hyperventilate.

    Fingers weaved into her hair before combing along the tangled tresses with a gentle 'shush'. Rio peppered her face in kisses until Agatha had relaxed in her hold again. "When I go back to work, you need to stay with somebody. Wanda, Alice, Jeff. Anybody that can keep an eye on you."

    Agatha kept her mouth shut. She wouldn't risk losing the one person she needed because she decided to run her mouth. She nodded into the shoulder. "Don't stay too long. They'll be calling for us soon."

    Rio looked at her with those sad, sad hazel eyes. She deserved answers, but she wasn't ready for the truth. The Ancient Ones wanted to punish Death far more than they did Agatha. Better to play the long game and protect her wife's feeling for once. It was the least she could do for the handful of centuries Death had protected her.

    Agatha hesitantly pressed a kiss to those lush lips that tingled with arcane magic. She sighed into it, eyes sliding shut. She would be okay now. Rio was here. Rio made everything better.

    By the time Alice knocked on her door, Rio had been whisked away to do her job. (An old apartment building had finally collapsed under its own weight. There would be few survivors.) Agatha put her coat on before following the witch down the stairs. It was funny how quickly the mind could forget details. How you could think an object was one color, but it was actually another. For example, she'd forgotten the exact shade of her son's hair. Or the fact that she had multiple now until Timothy Tommy had called her a variant of 'Ma'.

    Then there was the issue of the mortal couple staying in her house. She knew them, she just couldn't quite recall their names. They had something to do with William. That much, she remembered. They hovered around the boy but kept their eyes on her. She must have done something to piss them off.

    She'd worry about the strangers in her house later. The Ancient Ones would call on her son. They'd want to see their handiwork. Would want her degraded and humiliated. She needed to prepare accordingly. Needed to pull herself together before the others noticed anything. "You gonna come sit down?" Alice asked her. Child of Lorna, Agatha reminded herself. The young girl who survived a particularly nasty generational curse. She was wearing a police uniform now. That had to be recent.

    Agatha glanced over at the table of food. When had all of that gotten there? She never even heard the others shift about. Crap! Had she been standing in the middle of the room the whole time? She slunk over to the head of the table and eyed it warily.

    "Who cooked?" she asked to buy herself some time. Agatha still wasn't sure if she needed to eat. One month was a long time to go without a meal. Then again......

    "Jeff and I," Wanda told her with a gentle smile. "We made your favorites." Nicholas patted the spot to his left. The head of the table.

    Sitting in such a spot usually meant you played a key role in a group. But Agatha was a covenless witch by admission from half the group staring at her. Then again, it was her dearest son asking for her to take the spot. She was certainly a key figure in their coven of two.

    Agatha grabbed the sides of the chair cautiously before slipping into the seat. She'd need to find a new way to test her surroundings. Those eyes were watching her again. It reminded her of the Road. The distrust. The malice. The horrors.

    A small, missing piece clicked into place.

    "Mama, I missed you," the small child told her. He gave her a toothy grin. The goddess forced a grin. Why did he look older? She couldn't remember how she'd gotten him back. Couldn't recall him ever looking this healthy before. "I missed you too," she breathed. Always honest, even if he could be fake. Even if this was all some cruel dream. "Don't cry," he soothed. Nicholas handed her a napkin. "You're home now." She wanted to scream that nothing felt like home. She didn't know the meaning of the word. Not anymore. The damned Ancient Ones had robbed her of that. They'd taken more than her agency. More than her magic. She nodded rather than trying to find a way to twist the truth. "The Avengers swatted the school," Tommy, the other Maximoff, told her. "You missed Ma fuckin' goin' at 'em with her magic. She was all..." The teen started making fighting and explosion noises with his mouth as he punched the air.

    "Settle down," the witch laughed. "It wasn't that impressive."

    Agatha nodded, trying to recall her final moments before her punishment had been enacted. Why was Wanda even in her house? Wasn't she dead? She cut through her omelet with more focus than necessary.

    "You were so cool, Mama! I didn't know you could do that. Mother didn't either," Nicholas exclaimed.

    She stopped cutting as the memory of her final battle slotted into place. She'd killed Stephen Strange. She'd broken the biggest rule in a fit of rage. That was why the Ancient Ones had punished her so harshly. Agatha gripped the cutlery tightly as she forced herself to breathe evenly.

    "I didn't know I could do that either," she said, voice too high even for her. "I guess I'm just that powerful." Agatha forced herself to take a bite of the yellow egg. It tasted like sand in her mouth.

    "You totally kicked ass," Tommy laughed. "Fuckface Metal Man was crying in the parking lot like a wuss." The teen grinned from ear to ear as he bobbed his head in pride. There was something endearing in his manic energy. He reminded her of Rio.

    "Yes, thank you," she managed to say without choking. "What about the boyfriend, William? Where have you hidden him?"

    "Ah," the teen looked down at his plate. "Teddy is lying low with his parents. He was the one who told us our place was getting raided." William shifted. "If it weren't for Wanda and Jen, we wouldn't have been able to grab what we needed before coming over here." Agatha mulled over the words. Teddy was the boyfriend. Strange was dead. The Avengers were still after Wanda. The strangers were here for their safety. How was it they hadn't been ambushed by now? That was one of the few things that didn't quite make sense to her. She hummed with a nod. "Yes, very sensible." Agatha wasn't sure what else to say. William's mortals were looking at her again. This time, they didn't seem angry. They seemed confused. "Are you...feeling alright?" the woman asked. "You seem..."

    Agatha quickly plastered a grin on her face. "What? Me? 'Course I feel alright! I'm back with my best buds." She leaned over to Nicholas and nodded almost manically. Her son made a face, pulling back.

    Oh! Oh shit... She didn't use this mask with the group. That wasn't good. No wonder they were all staring at her as if she had a hole in her head. She probably looked like a complete psychopath. Agatha shoved her plate away as her stomach flipped with the realization. "Well, I better go shower," she laughed. The goddess pushed her chair back and stood with a clap. "Great job team. Really proud of you all!" She shook a fist before swinging it in a go- get-em gesture. Agatha flourished her coat before retreating upstairs.

    "I think it's safe to say Agatha's officially lost it," Jen told the group. "But Rio asked us to look after her, so that's what we'll do."

    The table eyed the empty chair. Suddenly, it was more than just a person that was missing. The witch had always had an air about her. One that was a testament to her age and wisdom (or as much wisdom only Agatha Harkness could possess). It was missing even though the body had returned. The house felt colder for it. Felt empty.

    Billy stabbed at his food, thinking.

    Rio had said she was summoned for breaking a rule which naturally meant a 'lecture' had taken place. But for a whole month was insanity. Time probably works differently in whatever space the Ancient Ones are in, he told himself. That was likely the reason why she was so dazed and confused now. It could have been minutes over there. But if that was the case, why did she act like she'd been gone for longer?

    "Who wants to take first shift with Agatha?" Jen asked the table. "Other than you, Gulliver." She sent a wink in her girlfriend's direction. The goth blushed.

    "I will," Nicky said, raising a hand.

    "You can be with her, but she needs an adult. Your mother's orders." The witch offered a smile to the boy. "Anyone else?"

    "I'll go first," Wanda told them. "Maybe I can get a little information. Find out what's going." Billy tuned the group out and reached out with his mind. He found Agatha's, but she must have had it firmly shut away. All he found was darkness when he peered into his mother's mind. Darkness and nothingness. It wasn't how she usually blocked a person, but she could have learned a new trick. She could move planets now. He didn't rule the possibility out. He'd find a way to talk to her about everything. He had to.

The maiden who had been dealt a bad hand but refused to let it hold her back. The mother who loved her sons unconditionally. And now, the crone who passed along her wisdom after having spent so many years in search of it.

    "I had my heart broken by a dead woman," Rio told her. "But I haven't seen her in some time. You, on the other hand, need only apologize for not greeting me with a kiss like you always do."

    Agatha beamed; blue eyes impossibly soft. She held the quant in one hand as she carefully leaned forward to give Death a kiss. The Divine Mother laughed breathily as she pulled back. "Sorry, my love. I was excited to see you."

    Death reveled in the flush tinting her wife's cheeks. "You're not afraid of getting in trouble so soon?"

    The goddess gripped the quant tightly before exhaling slowly. "They never explicitly said I couldn't visit you." She straightened herself before pushing them off once more. "And if I get in trouble, it'll have been worth it. You have Mrs. Tejedora back. It's the least I could do." Death hummed, a lazy grin stretching her cheeks. "My fearless champion," she teased. Agatha sent her a wink. "So, I'm having a grand old time, but where the hell is the shore?" The goddess looked around with furrowed brows. "I feel like I've been rowing forever." Just as she finished talking, the boat hit the bank. Agatha lurched forwards landing on her wife. The goddess licked her lips as pressed another kiss to Death's lips. "I'd like to get off now," she purred.

    Rio fluttered her lashes as her gaze dropped back down to her wife's mouth. It would be so easy to 'frisk' Agatha right here, right now. But alas! Death still had much to do, and so little time. She tucked a loose strand of hair behind Agatha's right ear, tucking a flower in place. "You'll have to wait," she sighed. Death grew a grin, slow and lazy. "This was fun though. We haven't helped each other in a while."

    Agatha pulled Rio up and helped her step out of the boat. She stuffed her hands in the folds of her robes with a cheeky grin. "Well, I'd say this date was a success!" "Date?" Rio sent her a look. "I was working."

    "Yeah, a work date," her wife said in agreement with a hurried nod. "Which is still a date!" Agatha tugged Death by her hips and pulled the cosmic entity in for a kiss. "Te veo," she drawled teasingly. Agatha stepped away with wink.

    Death practically swooned as the goddess flickered out of the Ether. "Mrs. Tejedora, I really fucking love my wife." The cosmic entity tossed her head back laughed loudly. Chapter End Notes Since Rio only interacted with her spider for less than five seconds in the show, we're gonna pretend it wasn't there so Agatha could retrieve her because I think it would be very cute and romantic of her.

    Tejedora translates to 'weaver' because tarantulas weave thick, chaotic bundles of silk.

    A-sleep...In...Bed...

    Chapter Summary Agatha is one busy woman.

    Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes Her first date a success, Agatha returned home with a spring in her step. She checked in on Nicky, pressed a kiss to his cheek, before going down the hall to her bedroom. The darkness of night had finally abated enough for her to lay her head down and sleep. Or she would have if there wasn't a snoring body lying under her covers.

    Agatha peeked at the intruder. "Tommy? What're you doing in my bed?"

    Her son popped his head up with a groggy, "Wha?"

    Poor thing had drool all across his cheek...and her pillow. "Go back to sleep," she told him quietly. Ugh, she was getting soft! Agatha tucked him back in and snuck downstairs. The others wouldn't be up for a couple of hours, so she could nap on her recliner. Maybe it would still smell like Rio.

    The Divine Mother snagged a blanket from the linen closet before popping out the footrest. (The couch did not smell like Rio anymore. Pity.) Agatha snuggled in and let her eyes drift shut.

    It was dark.

    It was quiet.

    Agatha tried to scream, to hear her own voice, but even that was soundless. There were no vibrations in the air as the goddess tried to move. She couldn't feel her body. She had no body. She had nothing.

    There was nothing.

    She was nothing.

    Agatha nearly jumped out of her skin as a hand shook her awake. She clutched at her blanket as she heaved lungfuls of air. Her face felt cold and wet. Was it sweat? Was it tears? Her heart raced regardless.

    Good. That was good. That meant she was alive.

    Wanda shushed her gently. She softly patted Agatha's shoulder. Her green eyes drifted all over her, checking her. "It's okay," Wanda whispered. "You're okay."

    The goddess fought tooth and nail to slap her walls up, but she couldn't find the strength. Agatha closed her eyes with a shudder. She hated the darkness that came with the prolonged blink. "I'm fine," she rasped. "You can go now."

    The stubborn woman shook her head with a laugh. "Come on, silly. Let's get you in a real bed."

    "Tommy's in mine," she informed hoarsely. Agatha produced a handkerchief and cleaned her face. She then blew her nose before tossing the silk square aside.

    The Kaplan's, who had been awoken by her nightmare, offered a small smile. "We'll handle breakfast," one of them said. (Agatha didn't care to see who.)

    Wanda thanked them as she steered Agatha towards her bedroom. "Come on, sleepy head. All good deities are resting up for a long day of work."

    Agatha scowled but climbed into the bed. "This never happened."

    The witch laughed. "Of course, it didn't, Agatha. Now, lay your head down." She crawled under the covers on the opposite side but remained sitting up. "Do you want to talk about it, or just sleep?"

    What would it hurt to tell the woman about her punishment now that the cat was out of the bag? The worse thing she could do was tell Rio, but she had to believe Wanda wasn't so ridiculously naive and foolish. Agatha made herself comfortable while avoiding those green eyes.

    "There was nothing."

    "Well, you'll have to talk to someone one of these days."

    Agatha scoffed. "No, you dolt. That's what I experienced. The nothingness that comes with true death. The emptiness of oblivion."

    Wanda gasped, just as dramatic as Billy. "That sounds awful!"

    She hummed, pulling the covers tight against her. "It's the worst thing I've ever experienced, and I've experienced a lot." Agatha shifted onto her side, a nail scratching at the plain pillowcase. "You can't feel or hear or see or do anything. Just darkness forever. It's a real bummer."

    A hand settled in her hair. Nails scratched lightly at her scalp. They didn't bring the arousing tingles like Rio's did, but it soothed her, nonetheless. Agatha let out a heavy sigh as tension ebbed from her shoulders.

    "When will you tell Rio?"

    Agatha felt tears sting her eyes at the thought. "She would be furious. I can't hurt her with that."

    A finger gently swiped an errant tear away. "It hurts her more to not know. She's probably telling herself they did unspeakable things to you." Wanda waved a hand, magic settling into the walls. "I've put up a small hex if you want to say anything you don't want them to hear." Agatha looked up at her, brows furrowing. "How did you...?"

    "Rio told us after you...uh...overworked yourself." Wanda gave her a tight smile. "She's upset, yes, but she's more worried than anything else. I think you should tell her when you're ready." "They want me to hurt her," Agatha whispered. "That's why they go after Nicky and I. They hate her." And now she knew why. Eternity wanted Rio to suffer for making him suffer. He was just like Agatha had been.

    Agatha nodded again. She felt the magic dissipate as she stared blankly forward. Wanda continued to stroke her head. "Get some sleep. I'll be right here." Agatha closed her eyes and faced the darkness.

    Nicholas knocked on Wanda's door with a grin. Downstairs, the smell of food was drifting upwards. He knew the woman would be hungry if she didn't come down soon. The child waited a few more moments before pushing the door open.

    Rio pressed a kiss to a gash to the side of Agatha's face. Her brows furrowed. "Did she say how she got these?" She looked over at the women. They were all covered in her wife's blood. Jen, it seemed, had the most on her pajama shirt.

    Wanda shook her head, drawing the Green Witch's attention. "No. She was too panicked when we found you two." She hugged herself, eyes red.

    Death turned away from the group and healed another scrape with an open-mouthed kiss. She smacked her lips, tasting the residual magic. It was arcane and reeked of the Ancient Ones. Her nostrils flared. "If those fuckers hurt her!"

    "...She did say she should have fought...them...harder," Alice mumbled. Rio hugged her unconscious her wife to her, kissing a temple. Was it not enough toying with her they had to harm her physically, too? "Los haré sufrir, mi corazón. Lo prometo." Rio laid her wife back against the armrest. "She'll be out for a while. Keep the hex up if you're able to, Wanda."

    Tommy shuffled forward; head bowed. "Is she gonna be okay, Ma Cubed?"

    "She will be, she'll just be tired. My dagger was in her for a long time." If Agatha was awake, Rio imagined a lewd joke would have been made. It made her lips twitch upwards slightly. "I'll get her bathed and put to bed. She'll be pissed if we ruin the sheets." (And another joke could have been made.)

    Death picked Agatha up, carrying her bridal style, and left with her wife.

    Billy stared at the bloodstain marring the sectional. "Is that something we can wash out?" he asked Alice.

    "We can magic it out, but it'll take several tries. She bled a lot." The goth waved him over. "Leave that to Wanda. We need to get dinner set up before anyone else passes out." He looked back as Wanda stared at the mess with hands on her hips. "Why don't all of you go shower? You're all dirty...No offense."

    Alice looked down at herself with a sigh. She examined her hands and the red staining them. "You're right...Can you get the hot pot started? I'll be back in like ten minutes." "Don't worry about me," he told her. "Dad can help me. He's the kitchen witch." Billy waved his father over. "I'll work on the hot pot; you steam the dumplings."

    Jeff jogged over and followed Billy into the kitchen. "I hope Agatha's okay. The living room looks like a war zone." He pulled out the bamboo steam baskets they had bought earlier and set them up. "How are you holding up, bud?"

    Billy took a moment to gather his thoughts as he turned the stove up to its highest setting. "Rio's not all that worried, so I think it'll be okay." He turned to face his father and swallowed thickly. "Do you think I'll ever get used to it? Seeing people hurt?"

    His father's eyebrows shot up. "Billy, I love you...but I hope you never get used to that sort of thing. That back there," he said as he jutted a thumb at the living room. "That was horrific. Nobody, especially not a teenager, should ever be okay with what just happened." The Kaplan-Maximoff-Harkness-Vidal shifted before bracing himself against the kitchen island. "On the Road...there was one more woman with us. Her name was Sharon Davis. She...she died."

    His first victim.

    A kind older woman who just wanted to attend a party. Billy had carried that guilt with him since the moment he found her staring up lifelessly. He had thought burying her would be paying her memory enough respect, but it continued to eat at him. Staying in the basement for nearly two months hadn't helped him much either.

    Billy flinched as a hand settled on his shoulder. His father eased him into a hug and sniffled. "I'm sorry you had to go through that," Jeff whispered. "I'm sorry your mother and I weren't there to help you understand anything."

    The teen clung to his father with a hiccup. They stood there in silence as Billy let himself feel his emotions.

    Someone cleared their throat.

    The duo looked over to find Rebecca staring at them with all the love in her heart. "Any chance I can get in on the action?"

    Billy laughed, sniffling. "Come on in. The water's just fine," he joked. Jeff and Rebecca bracketed their son, holding him tightly.

    Rio lay next to her wife and watched the scarlet of Wanda's magic pulse throughout the walls of the house. Agatha snored away; face still too pale for her liking. Death traced her facial features with a feather-light touch. The goddess' face twitched at the sensation before she snuggled closer.

    Every year since the birth of mankind, Death had played the part of rising and falling with the sun. It had never bothered her before. Then again, her ritualistic killing was still a fairly new practice. And once it had been initiated by the panel of women at her brother's behest, Stevie had always delivered a quick, merciful blow.

    Now, a cold, sinking feeling settled in Death's bones.

    Having Agatha deliver the blow didn't hurt her physically. It didn't even hurt her magically or spiritually. It had, however, hurt her emotionally. Her inability to prepare her wife for the ancient tradition had likely scarred Agatha. And if the coven's reaction was anything to go by, her wife had suffered greatly for her role in the ritual.

    Morbidly, Death wondered what everything had to have looked like once they returned home. What they must have thought as Agatha appeared with Death's dagger buried in a shoulder and Death's body in a coma? They all must have been terrified and confused. As the smell of food filled the air, Rio begrudgingly woke her wife. As much as the woman needed sleep, she needed food and water more. Agatha would just have to grumble her way through their meal before she could nap. "Levántate, mi amor~," she sang. Agatha stirred with a groan. Her eyelids fluttered before widening. "Ow..." Rio chuckled as she stroked a cheek. "Next time, don't get distracted," she teased. Rio wiggled her fingers with a small nose scrunch. "Up and at 'em, my love." The goddess pouted. "Don't want to."

    "Well, your coven worked very hard, so the least you can do is eat for a few minutes." She pressed a kiss to her wife's face. "Come on. I'll be there the whole time." "Don't you have to work?"

    Death ran a hand through auburn locks. She lost herself in the silky feel for a second. "It's not advised. I may have been reborn, but my magic takes a pretty big hit." Agatha sat up with a grunt. "Well, let me know before you take off. Pretty sure they're gonna kill me this time."

    Death tilted her head, brows furrowing as her smile faded. "Why did they hurt you?" She thumbed at a spot that had been cut up earlier. "Wasn't it good enough for them?" Agatha swallowed thickly. "I...may or may not have taken leave...without their permission..." Oh... Oh, the goddess was bound to be in serious trouble now. "You did what?! They would have let you out once they realized I was out cold." She stood to pace before turning back to the bed. "They'll take you from me. They will. I know it."

    The goddess curled in on herself, face gaunt. "So, I take a nap for a few more months. That's no big deal."

    Rio growled. "It isn't always about you and what you want, Agatha. Have you forgotten your coven? Your sons?" She grabbed Agatha by the arms and pulled her from the bed. "Have you forgotten about me?"

    "Why do you think I put up with any of their bullshit?" Agatha tried to wrench herself out of her wife's grasp. "I want our family to be safe. I want Nicky to grow up and have all of his magic. Damnit, Rio!"

    Agatha stopped pulling away and threw herself in Death's arms. "I didn't mean to kill them. Not Neva. Not my coven, or Alice. Hell, I didn't even mean to kill Strange. Not like anyone other than you would believe me." She sank to the floor, taking Rio with her. "I didn't ask to be cursed with my magic, or to fall in love with you and doom you even more than your job did. Now, everyone's in danger because I didn't think things through. I'm sorry!" Rio held her wife like she was a crumbling statue of sand. "Your magic is not a curse, Agatha. Stop telling yourself that. For once, believe me." She hugged the woman tightly, rocking her. "Your magic is beautiful! The way you love is beautiful. You are beautiful." "Why won't you hate me!"

    Death felt the heart in her chest clench agonizingly. When would Agatha Harkness learn to love herself? What would it take?

    Rio pulled Agatha into a chaste kiss. She reached down deep with her magic before centering it in her chest. In Agatha's heart. Her wife whimpered into the kiss.

    "What is that?"

    "My love for you," Death confessed. She shushed her goddess as she fed her magic and all her adoration. "Stop fighting it. You are loved, Agatha."

    Agatha clung to her as her sobs turned to sniffles. She twitched. "Stop...I can't...breathe..." Rio eased back slowly until she cut the witch off. "Did I hurt you?" She checked over her wife with her hands and eyes. Some color had returned to Agatha's face, but not enough for her to be fully recovered. "Agatha...Hey, talk to me!" Rio began to panic. Thankfully, her wife had just fallen back asleep. Death sighed, glad to have avoided another emergency situation for the time being.

    "Do you think they're going to come down or...?"

    Nicholas looked between Jen and Wanda. Now that the whole house had stopped shaking, the group wasn't sure if they should go back to cooking, or if they should check on the immortals. The boy set his batch of tangyuan down in the bowl with the others. "I'll go check," he told them. "Mother and Mama don't yell when I'm near."

    "I don't know," Wanda began. Her gaze drifted off to the side.

    "I'll be super quick," he promised. "I'll tell Mother the food's almost done." "Agatha does need to get up and drink something," Alice added. She gave the Scarlet Witch a look. "It's better we send him than someone they'll throw through a wall." "Fine," Wanda sighed.

    "Use your best pouty face," Alice instructed. "Really lay it on thick, okay?" Nicholas saluted the coven. "Be right back!"

Instead of laying in the bed like normal people, his mothers were situated on the floor with his mother's cloak thrown across his mama's body. Death raised her head to look at him. She looked just as tired as she always did on the Solstice.

    "Tired," the goddess whined. "Leave me."

    "But Mama," Nicholas said, pouting just as he'd been told. He even went so far as to sniffle and forced a tear to well in his left eye. (Just like his mother. Always crying from left to right.)

    "Nicky, not you too," the Divine Mother groaned. She buried herself deeper into Death's arms. "Let me sleep."

    The young Harkness-Vidal stomped a foot. "If you don't come down and eat with us, I'm gonna run away with Señor Scratchy!"

"Mama...You're not allowed to run away anymore," he told her seriously. "The others aren't as good at finding us as Mother is. You'll make them sad, and I don't want our family to be sad."

    Death hummed. "He makes a good point, Agatha. Even more reason to get up and come eat." "You both are so infuriating," his mama huffed. "Won't let me die. Won't let me sleep. Won't let me wallow in self-pity for five fucking minutes!"

    He nodded enthusiastically. "I love you this much," he told her before launching himself on top of the two. Nicholas kicked and giggled as two pairs of hands tickled his sides. "No, no," he shrieked shrilly. "You're supposed to tickle Mama."

    "Oh, my apologies!" Death joined him in making the Divine Mother squirm. "Keep going. I bet we can make her pee her pants."

    Death mirrored his grin. "Now, there's an idea!" She took in a large, dramatic breath in. "I'm going, damn you!" His mama popped up off the floor and hugged the cloak around her. "You two are grounded. No more tickle fights for either of you."

She hugged the cloak tighter to herself, focusing on the residual warmth from Death's 'You are loved!' stunt. Agatha pressed a hand to her chest as she lost herself in the afterglow.

    Rio chuckled as she walked up behind her. Her hazel eyes twinkled in the dim light before she picked her wife up. (Agatha was not swooning. Shut up. You saw nothing!) "You're so cranky when you're tired."

    Her wife loved her. Not a little bit of love. Not gratuitous amounts of lust for her body (of which Agatha would not have complained). Just pure, unadulterated love. The kind that Agatha had always wanted from the cosmic entity. She would make sure she remained worthy of that vast, maddening emotion. Agatha would shower Death with praise and a warm embrace right before laying a fresh set of bodies at her feet.

Her blood, Agatha noted, had stained the floor. They would need to replace it before inviting anybody over. The sectional was missing and would also need to be replaced. The Ancient Ones would be receiving a bill for the damages.

    Ugh! There's something I never thought I'd hear him say. Agatha looked around. "Do we have anything hard to drink? Preferably dark and aged."

    "You need water," Nicky told her sternly. "Alice said if you don't rehydrate, she's going to strap you down and force you to drink fifty gallons!"

    Alice fixed her with a look from her spot at the table. "We've got a whole coven who can tackle you to the ground in ten seconds. You're drinking water."

    Agatha hugged herself with a hum. "You're all getting way too comfortable bossing me around."

    "Water," Billy told her.

    "I think you've been outvoted," Rio teased. She set a glass down for her wife. "The things I put up with," she grumbled to herself. Agatha reached for her sad excuse of a drink and took a sip. "There. Happy?"

    "You haven't said why you didn't give me siblings, Mama."

    Damn Maximoff's! They'd taught her precious baby boy their annoying persistence. Agatha pursed her lips as she tried to come up with a good, honest excuse.

    "Oh, it was awful," Rio said, feigning a swoon. She pressed the back of her hand to her forehead with a dramatic groan. "Your Mama was so rude to me. Did you know she stopped taking my flowers?"

    "Okay, now you're going too far," Agatha warned with a rasp. "Let's pull it back before someone's feelings get hurt."

    "Can't get any more hurt than Rio's," Rebecca mumbled.

    "Hey!" The Divine Mother fumed. Who did they think they were!

    "Did you really tell Mother that! Why?" Nicky looked at her with a familiar, hurt expression. Unsurprisingly, Agatha saw it mirrored on the other side of the table. The ancient witch pursed her lips as she took a steadying breath. "I did tell her that-" "This was the face you fell in love with...I still don't understand what was so wrong with it," Rio told her, voice like glass. She lifted her hazel eyes to hold Agatha's gaze. All her irritation with the others gave way to the great sorrow of her heart despite the overpouring of love an hour ago. For the first time in months, Agatha reached for her brooch in search of comfort. She grimaced as she found empty air. Her gaze shifted back over to her right. Nicky looked at her expectantly.

    "You looked too much like him," she said, hollowly.

    In an instant, Agatha was back in the woods of Salem. The smell of decaying soil was now replaced by the scent of her own salty tears and sweat. If she looked down, she'd find Nicky's burial. Fresh and vibrant with sprigs of lavender. Still with her son, but so terribly alone. "So, you think I'm beautiful," Nicky said, preening.

    Agatha blinked. "Like a prized rose," she affirmed. Agatha reached out and cupped his chin with an easy smile. (He was still real.)

    There was a set of sniffles from Billy and Alice.

    "That's the most heartbreaking, gayest shit I've ever heard," the teen told her. "It's not cool at all, but I totally get it."

Like she hadn't just admitted that she was so overcome with grief that she pushed her wife away for simply having the stronger genetics.

Rio leaned heavily against the table as she stared up at her wife. She'd never hidden her lovesick expression before, but now it was like being stabbed (affectionately) all over again. Agatha looked away, fighting a fully- body blush. Damn bleeding hearts!

    Agatha stormed over to the bathroom door. "If one of you doesn't turn that shit off!" She slammed the door shut with a wordless shout. Agatha decided, in that moment, that she hated the holidays.

    Now that Yule and Dongzhi had passed, the coven was being irritatingly insufferable with their obnoxious, eardrum bursting, brain-bleed inducing cacophony of sounds and screeching. Agatha had managed to avoid the noxious 'music' for the past century, but now? Now, she had to endure it until the coven went to sleep. Three days from the 25th, and the nonsense had still started way too soon. Agatha was more than ready to strangle every person in the house.

    Even Nicky, for all his adorableness, had succumbed to the mind-numbing gibberish of Christmas carols and, dare she even think the name! The... She was gonna be sick. The Muppets: A Christmas Carol!

    Oh! The horror!

    Agatha dreaded the people her coven would become in a few days. At least the Kaplan's were above the embarrassing display of capitalistic debauchery. They had gotten simple gifts for the coven to open after Temple. Eight nights' worth, and all in tiny, neat packaging. (Agatha had attempted a peak, but Billy had a magical alarm system in place. The traitor.) Wanda knocked on the bathroom door. She yawned loudly, as she had been doing for the past day, before stepping into the small room. "Now that you're feeling better, I'm gonna drop the hex and nap."

    Agatha finished stepping into the shower to frown freely. "Can you at least let me finish cleaning myself off? I'm not keen on flashing Them Upstairs."

    "I thought you welcomed any and all flirtation from women," Wanda said with a laugh. Agatha heard the toilet lid click and shift as the woman took a seat.

    "Not from them I don't," she said without much thought. Wanda, it seemed, had found a way to become Agatha's confidant. It was infuriating and more helpful than the goddess ever cared to admit.

    "What do you mean? Well, I suppose you wouldn't enjoy people flirting with you after torturing you."

    Agatha huffed out a laugh as she rinsed her hair out. The water tinged a faint pink as the last of her blood was washed away. "No, I'm perfectly fine with that, dear. But with them...With them, it's pure harassment." The Divine Mother hugged herself, fingers pressing to Rio's initials. "I've been with them all, and now they think they own me."

    "That's awful," she heard Wanda breathe. "Agatha, I'm so sorry. That was a terrible thing for me to say."

    The goddess smirked. "It's okay. I'm only crying in a shower," she lied easily. Agatha even sniffled and blew her nose for added effect.

    Wanda ripped the curtains open, guilt written on her face. She scowled at the snickering woman and magicked the water to be ice cold. "That was terrible of you." "Oh, honey, I could have said much worse." Agatha grinned wider as Wanda reacted to the immortal's body's reaction to the cold. "See somethin' you like, sweetums?" "No," Wanda huffed, mortified and only slightly scarred. Or was it aroused? Agatha could never tell with the woman.

    She shut the water off before stepping closer to the woman. Agatha could feel the hum of chaos magic around them. Herself, she was starving for a real magical hit! All that tasty, juicy magic just begged to be taken. If only Wanda were so easily pliable as to give her weekly doses, but alas...

    "You just gonna stand there staring, or are you gonna hand me a towel?" Agatha threw her hands up with a smirk as Wanda jerked away in search of the clearly, obviously fresh towel resting on the counter behind her.

    Wanda fumbled with the folded, fluffy towel before handing it off. Perhaps it was arousal after all. How long could a woman go without being touched? Agatha had never dared to try and find out. There would have been so many more murders if she had.

    "I have a strong feeling I might be taking an extended leave of absence," Agatha began. She toweled off as the dread began to settle back in the pit of her stomach. Twenty-four hours was a long time for her and Rio to hide in a hex. "After you drop the hex, keep the coven indoors...just in case."
