    Had she? Evanora Harkness' most cruel predictions had all come true in some form of fashion. But if that was the case, then Agatha really was evil incarnate. She really was a lost cause and her the coven was wasting their time on her.

    "What have you done to yourself?" her mother snarled. "You cannot entice me with a young face full of tears."

    "Mother...," she sobbed. But she had nothing to say in her defense. Not anymore. Agatha raised her head to stare at the woman and found only disgust. She thought back on her life. How she had never seen anything but two emotions on the woman's face. "...I would have never been enough for you."

    "You would never be enough for anyone."

    "Agatha," Rio growled, drawing her attention. "Don't listen to her." Death shifted her gaze to glare at the ghost. "You're the only one lacking in this room. Not her." Agatha pressed a hand to her chest where her heart lay. Rio, her wife and mother of her child, had given it a purpose. Agatha was loved and could love. She set her jaw, eyes hardening. The last of her young heart, her love for her mother, fell away. The goddess rose, her magic crackling.

    "Put that away. You wouldn't know what to do with it." Evanora threw her into a wall. Agatha righted herself with a laugh. She thumbed at her busted lip with a scoff. "Haven't you heard, Mother? I'm a god now." Her lips twisted as she cleared the clutter around her. "Go on. Send a prayer my way. Let's see if you're worth answering." She waved a hand, a circle enclosing the two witches.

    "What are you doing?" Jen hissed. "You're supposed to be on the other side of the chalk." "Agatha, don't," her wife growled. "You won't be able to come back if you do." "It's a risk I'm willing to take." She turned to face her coven, shoulders squared. "It is my duty as coven leader to expel the evil that threatens them." Agatha turned back to Evanora. "Let me show you evil so you never get the meaning confused again."

    "Come on, Agatha. Just...Just step over the line," Wanda pleaded. "We'll find another way to banish her. Please, Agatha!"

    "Ugh," she scoffed. Bleeding hearts...would she ever be free of them? (She never wanted to be free of them.) Agatha graced the woman with a smirk. "Today's lesson will be over banishing a vengeful spirit."

    "Stop," Billy told her firmly.

    "This is especially important when a bunch of old biddies have hexed a soul for punishment purposes." Agatha looked up at the ceiling, rage rolling off of her in waves. Her purple lashed out but stayed within the bounds of the spell circle. Evanora cried out as the magic whipped her about. "The coven must hold hands."

    "Do you know what you're doing?" Rio asked her. "Are you okay with losing your connection to this realm?"

    "Mama, don't leave me," Nicky pleaded. His bottom lip trembled as he clutched at Rio. Agatha swallowed thickly. As long as the Ancient Ones had tethered her mother Evanora to her, the spirit would be able to return again and again. And if Evanora could return, her family would be in constant danger. The banishment had to include her. "Trust me," she told her wife. Agatha needed Rio to have faith in her for the coven's sake. If they could have faith in her, she could have faith in herself.

    Death clenched her jaw before nodding. "Hands," she told the others. Rio took Nicky's and Alice's. The rest followed suit until they formed a circle around the pair. They stayed away from the spell circle, faces twisted into wet messes of tears and snot. Her coven... Evanora laughed brazenly. "You are incapable of banishing me for I am not evil. They will succeed only in banishing you, Agatha."

    Agatha turned her attention back to the ghost. "Play me off, Billy. You know the words." She flourished her sleeves, ready to aid in their prayers. Agatha looked around at the group one more time. They would be directionless without her. Would be emotional wrecks. She took a page from her wife's book and fed her magic into the foundation of her house. Fed all of her love and loyalty and gratitude to the wood and concrete and carpet. Let it seep into every nook and cranny.

    Even if she never found her way back, her magic would keep them safe. Would make sure that Evanora never threatened her family ever again.

    "Vale ad lucum," Billy began, voice strained. He broke into a sob, his spell book falling shut. "Oh, come on. You can do better than that," she told him. "Be a witch. This isn't my first funeral."

    Evanora sneered at her. She lashed out, pining her to the floor. "Do you really think you can go against the will of the Universe?"

    Agatha cackled as she shoved the woman into the bounds of the spell circle. "You think I give a shit what those five want?" She threw her head back for another laugh. "I am the Divine Mother. The Triple Goddess. My will be done." She advanced on the spirit, her magic crackling sharply. "All you had to do was be a good mother," she snarled. Agatha pointed to Billy. "Go. Again. And don't stop this time."

    Her son stared at her with wide eyes. He fumbled with his spell book before doing as he was told. "Vale ad lucum, relinque terram, noli esse phantasma...Vale ad lucum, relinque terram, noli esse phantasma..."

    The others joined him, their voices blending. The words were stated strongly. Firmly. The white chalk began to glow a deep blue.

    "The Ancient Ones will not be pleased with you," Evanora told her. "They will punish you worse."

    "Oh, I'm counting on it," Agatha sneered. "But they've already done too much and gone too far." She laughed darkly as they began to flicker. "I think I'll start with you, Mother." Her voice dripped with malice, with murderous intent as the floor opened up to a world of white. "Shall we?"

    They were encapsulated by the light before being pulled down into whatever Goddess- forsaken realm. The floor closed up behind them. The candles extinguished themselves.

    Nicholas ran to his mother, hugging her tightly. "You have to take me to Mama. You have to!"

    "No, querido. You mama wouldn't want that." She tried to calm him, hands wiping away his tears. "You cannot go where she has gone."

    He pulled away from her, tears stinging his cheeks. "Bring her back! Do it. Bring Mama back," he demanded.

    "Nicky," his mother said, voice cracking. "Nicky, I can't. She's banished. I can't bring her home. She's on the Other Side, like you were."

    Nicholas shook. "I wish I never came back!" He shoved his way past the others and ran to his room. Nicholas slammed the door shut before throwing himself on his bed. He hugged his stuffed rabbit to his chest as his lower lip trembled. The stitching drank up his tears as he cried himself to sleep.

    Agatha and Evanora found themselves surrounded by endless white. Unlike Oblivion, the goddess could still sense things. Could still hear her unwanted companion's growling. She rounded on the woman, her robes swishing. "Now, I get to do my part." Agatha grinned wolfishly.

    "You have no magic here," Evanora argued. "We are in the realm of the gods, child." "I know we are," Agatha laughed. She sneered, all pretenses dropping, and pressed her fingertips together. "I wasn't lying earlier. You made sure I never exaggerated with you." She ignited her hands with magic and reveled in the panic that alighted Evanora's eyes. "I would have forgiven you," she confessed, "but you've made up your mind. So now, I'll make up mine."

    "I don't want your forgiveness, nor your love," the spirit sniffed. "You are nothing to me. Nothing."

    "I'm glad we're finally on the same page then." Agatha inhaled deeply, drawing her power until she felt the tingle in the roots of her hair and the tips of her toes. "How's a little trip down memory lane sound?"

    A stake made of pure magic appeared.

    Agatha bound Evanora to it. She circled the witch, salivating. "I was going to destroy your magic with a spell, but I think I'll give you the same treatment you gave me. Condemned. Begging. I wasn't a monster then. There was still time, but you didn't find me worthy of saving. No one will save you here."

    Wood sparked into existence at the bound witch's feet. Agatha would question who had summoned it later. She was just grateful for the kindling.

    "I've a twist, of course. I'm not going to taint my power reserves with yours again." Agatha tutted as she conjured a flame in her palm. She held it for a moment. Felt the heat against the side of her face. "I know letting you recycle would be the best form of punishment, but I just can't stand the thought of you taking another breath." Agatha tossed the flame onto the wood stack. She stuffed her hands in the folds of her robes, smirking.

    "You're evil," Evanora snarled. "You will always be evil."

    Agatha chuckled darkly. "I know. That's why my wife loves me so much," she said with a saccharine sweet tone. "I'll be sure to name a dog after you. Then, I'll hit it with a car just for good measure. In fact, I'll make it a yearly tradition. My bastard, abomination children will sing songs of your ultimate demise."

    The flames licked up Evanora's legs. She cried out, trying to pull away. The flames turned black with an acrid smell.

    "Whoops! Looks like you're just as tainted as I, Mother." Agatha cackled, a hand pressing to her chest. "Dearie me, but you're starting to make quite the racket. I wish I had some wine to go with the view. It would be nice to have Rio, too. She'd kill for a chance to watch you burn."

    The witch shrieked, her voice echoing into the bright nothingness. Her body writhed as her soul twisted and fell apart.

    Agatha watched the flames grow higher. Her grin grew with it. The part of her that had been branded evil was giddy at the sight. "I don't think I've ever seen anything more divine than a ruin of my own doing."

    Save me, Evanora prayed silently. Her screams like a symphony to her dying melody. "Save you? Why would I save you?" Agatha laughed, doubling over slightly. Her face twisted with malice. With rage. With centuries of anguish. "You brought this on yourself. I didn't tell you to hate your child."

    "Goddess! Hear me," Evanora pleaded. Her face bubbled and turned black as the flames reached her neck. "Spare me this suffering!"

    Agatha shook her head. "You would deny me my position until the bitter end, wouldn't you? First, as a daughter. Then, as a coven member. And now, as the goddess I was always meant to be." Agatha waved a hand, lowering the temperature of the flames. "I'd rather hear you caterwaul for several more hours before I let you go. I want my pain to reduce you to ashes. I want you to anguish until there is nothing left to feel."

    Evanora screamed and sobbed, but Agatha would not let her die just yet. The witch threw her head back as the goddess forced her memories into the spirit's head. Let Evanora Harkness know the cruelties she had inflicted. Let Evanora Harkness beg for a savior just for it to be ripped away by her own doing. The ancient witch lowered on the stake, her cries steadily growing softer.

    Agatha watched with a smile on her face as the soul was splintered, torn, and shattered by the flames of her fury. The Ancient Ones were next. They'd gone too far in introducing her son to his grandmother. Had taken what little trust she had in Stevie and dashed it against the jagged peaks of betrayal. They would pay. They would be crushed under her heel. They would beg her forgiveness, and she would laugh in their faces.

    Agatha Harkness was done playing their games.

    She abandoned the woman. Abandoned the hurt in favor of anger.

    Agatha sniffed. "Ugh. I better not smell like this for the rest of my life." She waved a hand in front of her face in an attempt to clear the smell. "Where the hell am I?" Agatha looked around, blinking. "This is about to so fucking annoying," she huffed.

    Billy gave his twin and Teddy a look before pushing open the bedroom door. On the bed, Nicky sat with Señor Scratchy in his lap. The boy looked anything but happy. His eyes were red, and his mouth was set in a firm, unmoving frown. The teens carefully entered the bedroom, not wanting to further upset the child.

    "Hey," Billy greeted cautiously. "Teddy came to see you. He's about to head to the airport with his parents. We thought you might like to tell him 'bye'."

    Nicky dragged his gaze over to the trio. The Maximoff was struck by just how similar the scowl was to their shared mother's. "Hi," he huffed out. It wasn't quite a growl, but it certainly wasn't happy either.

    Teddy stepped past Billy and crouched in front of the boy. "You know, my parents have to fly out of town a lot. Growing up, I didn't see them much." He reached out and pet the rabbit with a small smile. "Now that I'm older, I get why they had to. Children mean a lot to their parents. Your mom...she made a sacrifice to keep you safe. She didn't leave you because she doesn't love you."

    "She didn't have to be in the circle," Nicky argued. "She shouldn't have to listen to her bosses. Their mean to her and Mother."

    Billy grimaced. "It's not that simple. Ma is only alive because she made a deal with them. If she doesn't do what they say, they'll really take her away forever."

    "But you brought me and Alice back. Why can't you just do that to Mama too?" "She doesn't have the same kind of soul as you." The teen shuffled, not sure how to elaborate. "But you're the Demiurge," Nicky huffed. "You can do anything." He scrunched his nose up before a dark look settled on his face. "You don't wanna save Mama, do you? You just wanted her all to yourself."

    "What?! No! I-"

    "I don't want you in here," the boy growled. "Go away. Go!"

    Señor Scratchy sensed the boy's anguish and turned on the trio. He growled like a rabid dog and forced them towards the door.

    Billy held his hands up as he backed away. "Nicky, stop!"

    His brother slid off the bed to face the door. A heavy scowl pulled his face down. His glare sent a chill down Billy's spine. "I wanna be alone!"

    A gust of wind slammed the door shut.

    The Maximoff stood in the hallway with his mouth hanging open.

    Agatha rubbed a temple.

    Banishments were no mystery to the witch. She'd had to send many a spectral enemy away over the years. What perplexed her was her own banishment. All rejected souls were meant to be sent straight to the Eternal Forests to wander for all eternity. It's where she should have gone, but she didn't. Why? She had no fucking clue. It likely had to do with whatever Billy and the Ancient Ones had done to her to make her the Divine Mother. The only problem was that she didn't even know what that was.

    Berkana. Perth. Ansuz. Kauna. Laguz Rebirth. Fate. God. Wisdom. Water. Those were the runes the Ancient Ones had bestowed upon her to create her a new, working soul. But Billy had still tampered with her original, mortal, cosmic make-up. Not even the Ancient Ones could fully remove what he'd done. Therefore...she still had no fucking clue as to where she was.

    Agatha did the only thing she could think of as she continued to stroll through the vastness of her new home. "There's a road that's wild and wicked, winding though the wood," she began singing.

    She missed her family. Had hoped to have a relaxing week with them for Hanukkah and Christmas. Agatha had even gotten everybody gifts. (They were stolen, but gifts none the less.) And she'd even set up a spell to decorate the house while everybody slept. Now, all her hard work would be appreciated, but she'd get none of the praise. It was a damn shame, if she was being honest with herself. All that mistletoe...

    "Down, down, down the road. Down the windy...er...Hello!" Agatha perked up as she noticed what looked (please Herself!) like another person. She rushed over, not caring how silly she might have looked. "Hey! Hey you!"

    The person turned to face her, a smile gracing her lips. "Oh...Hello, there," the woman said. Her voice was smooth like honey but carried the weight something ancient. The stranger had glowing tan skin, dark eyes, and curly raven hair that reached down to where the back of her knees would have been. She wore a flowing, golden toga that hid her feet from view. Agatha slowed to a stop with a pant. She bent over and braced herself against her knees as she caught her breath. She held up a finger, silently shushing the woman should she try to say anything. Once her heart rate had settled, Agatha straightened up with a flap of her robes. "You wouldn't happen to know where we are, would you? 'Cause I'd like to get out of here." The woman chuckled. She eyed the goddess for a moment. "You must be Agatha. What a fine specimen you've grown into. Although, you look younger than I imagined." She examined the witch a pleased hum.

    The Divine Mother smirked, never one to turn down a compliment. "Look, I'd love to stay and flirt with a pretty thing like you, but I really need to get home. I left the stove on." She jutted a thumb behind her with a huff. "Plus, I left like ten kids unattended." Her new companion (or was Agatha the new companion?) smiled wider. Her black eyes twinkled with something of a knowing look. "Ah...yes. Mustn't leave your coven to their own devices for too long, should we?" She held out a hand to the goddess. "May I?" Agatha eyed the hand with a tight grin. The gesture felt vaguely familiar. Like how the Ancient Ones had called her over to make her the Triple Goddess. "You're not going to kill me, are you? Not that I'd be opposed, you are very beautiful, but you'll have to get in line for that. Pretty sure my coven's called first dibs." She stuffed her hands into her pockets to keep them from shaking. Please, not another pretty-faced enemy. Can't I catch a break? The stranger laughed. The sound jingled merrily around them, stretching on forever. She smiled warmly before shaking her head gently. "No, my dear. I need to remove the banishment from your soul. Don't worry, though. I'll be gentle."

    The goddess took a single step forward as her brows furrowed. Agatha side-eyed the woman. "You sure are friendly. What's the catch? Keep in mind, I have a wife whom I love very much. I won't try to stop her if you make her jealous. It's very erotic when she's all angry and stabby."

    The witch, because she had to be one if she knew about her banishment, giggled. She crooked a finger, coaxing Agatha closer. "No catch, Divine Mother. I simply wish to help you get home."

    Agatha took the few steps forward and stopped an arm's length away. She flinched as the woman reached out to her.

    "Shh. I won't hurt you," the witch soothed. With a feather light touch, she pressed her palm against Agatha's sternum.

    Agatha did her best not to twitch. "Kinda tickles," she said with twisting lips. "Didn't know you could undo banishments."

    "Not everyone is as exceptional as we, Agatha Harkness." The hand was removed. "That ought to do it. I do hope we'll cross paths again, Your Excellency."

    Agatha preened. "Like-wise!" She yelped as the surface beneath her feet disappeared.

    Unlike her dismissal by the Ancient Ones, the mystery woman settled Agatha down ever so gently on the grass of her lawn. The goddess blinked slowly.

    "One white hell for another," she grumbled. Her bare feet crunched along the snow as she moved towards her front door.

    "Agatha?"

    She turned to face her visitor. "Thor? What are you doing here?" The submarine implosion! "You can't prove I did shit!"

    The Asgardian pulled a face. "That's not...Look, I came to exonerate you and your coven. You won't have any more Avenger spies tailing you."

    "Oh..." She flapped her arms, playing with the flowing sleeves. "That's good news to hear. You came all the way to this sleepy little town for that?"

    Thor chuckled. "I thought you'd like to hear it in person." He wrung his hands. "There's something else..."

    Agatha quirked a brow, intrigued. "Go on..."

    "My father is hosting a party for New Year's. He would like for you and Lady Death to attend," the blonde beefcake broached.

    "That's very kind of him. We'll happily pay Daddy Dearest a visit." Agatha cleared her throat. "Do you...wanna step inside for a glass of cider or something?"

    Thor gave her a tight grin. "I don't they'd like to see me on today of all days," he told her. "And you should be careful about hypothermia." He pointed to her feet. "Hurts something fierce for us immortals."

    "My wife's kisses always make me feel better," Agatha purred, just to make the Asgardian squirm. "Well, I look forward to meeting Odin in the flesh. I better get inside and let them know I'm home." She turned away at the same time the Avenger did. Agatha ignored the chill in her toes and entered her home.

    It was warm and smelled strongly of cinnamon and pine. Like burning cedar and roasted chestnuts. The house smelled like home to Agatha. It felt lived in and loved. She sniffled as a tear managed to escape in her moment of weakness. She'd really believed she'd never get to see her giant, gaudy house again. That she'd never hear her coven's voices nagging each other and ribbing her again. Agatha took a deep breath before closing the door loudly and dramatically.

    "Who left their shoes right in front of the door?" she shouted, a smirk on her lips. THUMP THUMP THUMP THUMP THUMP "Holy shit, it's a Christmas miracle," Tommy laughed. He threw himself at her before squeezing her tightly. "Welcome back, Ma Squared! I missed you."

    Oh, but Agatha was openly weeping at the honest and genuine emotion in her son's voice! She pressed a kiss to the crown of his head, sinking into his embrace. "I missed you too, kiddo."

    "Ma!" Billy skidded to a halt. He had flour on his face and the widest eyes she'd ever seen on him. "You're really back," he breathed. The teen joined the hug with a bruising grip on her arm. "You really are a slippery witch, aren't you?"

    "The slippery-ist!" She ruffled his curls before gracing him with a forehead kiss. "Now, I'm missing two kids. Where the hell is Nicky and Alice?"

    "Ah..."

    The twins fell quiet with matching grimaces.

    Agatha looked between the two, her heart racing. She gripped them tightly. "What? What happened? Are they hurt? Where are they?" Her head wobbled from side to side as she looked for signs of the two.

    "Alice is fine. She just had a shift today, but..."

    "Your son is mad at you," a voice said from the staircase. Rio leaned over the banister, her eyes burning into the goddess. She wore a black turtleneck and black leggings. A bad omen if Agatha knew one. (She looked damn hot in her outfit, though!) Her wife had been back to wearing her greens and browns, so an all-black outfit had to mean...

    Agatha shifted with a frown. "Mad at me? What did I do?"

    Billy looked away as Tommy scuffed his heel against the floor.

    "Come with me. You'll find out soon enough," Rio sighed. She stood up and held out a hand. Agatha gave the twins one last look before pressing forward. "What are you doing here? I figured you'd be at work or by my grave crying inconsolably," she said, hoping a joke would ease the somber expression on her wife's face.

    "It's Christmas and the first day of Hanukkah," Death informed. "And Nicky refuses to come down."

    The couple climbed the rest of the steps until they reached the second floor. Agatha found herself seeking Rio's hand as the cosmic entity knocked on the door.

    "Brace yourself. He's..." Rio sucked in a breath, frown deepening. "He's going through it." Agatha swallowed thickly. "But what do you-?"

    The door swung up, nearly tearing off its hinges. Nicky's room was a terror. Her once cherubic son now glared at her with a ferocity Agatha thought only Death capable of. The child, or preteen, more like, refused to utter a single word. Instead, a whirlwind erupted from nothingness and forced the door shut once more.

    Agatha turned to stare at her wife. "I think he's a little more than mad, my love." "He won't talk to anybody right now. I'm actually surprised he didn't let you in," Rio mumbled. "I'm going to guess he's too upset to have recognized you. You don't exactly look the same."

    The goddess was too busy trying to understand the situation to notice her wife's remark. Had she stopped to look in literally any mirror, Agatha would have seen that she had yet to return to her preferred age. But since she was preoccupied with more pressing matters, Rio didn't think to point it out any further. She'd realize on her own.

    "Well...I say we give him a moment. He'll be fine."

    "Agatha," Rio growled.

    "Okay, okay. Give me a minute to come up with something. I don't feel like walking into another warzone, okay? My body hurts like a bitch right now." Agatha wiggled her toes. "Are you going to help me or what?"

    Death rolled her eyes. "You can nap after you talk to Nicky. I need to pop out for a second." She stepped closer before pressing a kiss to her wife's lips. "Welcome home, my love." Agatha felt her cheeks warm. "Y'know, I'm gonna kill you."

    "What for?" Rio laughed.

    "You've turned me into a blushing bride again. It's disgusting," she sneered. "I'm a powerful witch. I don't swoon because your pretty face is near. It's...It's..." "Adorable," Death teased. "The Great Agatha Harkness, smitten with Death? I do believe it was written in the stars, my love."

    "Go," Agatha growled. "So, you can come back, and I can give you your gift." The cosmic entity's eyes widened. She smirked, licking her teeth. "Yes, ma'am!" Agatha swatted at her as a blush began anew.

Nicky would have been duped by the ancient witch regardless. The Ancient Ones had probably planned on it. (Those fucking bitches!)

"Well, from now on, no talking to strangers, mister. That was very dangerous. What if you had been kidnapped again, hm? Your mother and I would have had to rip the world apart to get you back." She hugged him tightly just to prove he hadn't been kidnapped. Agatha let out a breath, willing her anger to calm. "Nicky, do you swear she didn't hurt you?"

    "I swear, Mama. She was really nice until she saw you." He looked up at her. His face was scrunched up in that way Rio was known to do when she was displeased with something. It was as adorable as it was endearing. "I don't like the way she talked to you, Mama. You were right. She's not a good mother. And when she hurt you..." His tiny voice cracked. "I was scared for you."

Agatha cupped his cheek and softly added, "But I was more scared for you and the coven. The thing is...Mama can face any monster if it means keeping you safe. Even ones that make her really, really scared."

    Nicky threw his arms around her neck with a sob. "I love you, Mama."

    "I love you, too, Nicky."

    Rio stepped into her wife's bedroom to find the goddess pulling on a bland t-shirt. She cocked her head to the side as she caught a glimpse of the woman face.

    Agatha had yet to turn back to normal. Interesting. Perhaps she was stuck. Or her mother had just done a number on her wife's psyche. Maybe it was both. Maybe it was neither. "You're staring," Agatha laughed. She stepped past the cosmic entity to pull her shoes on. "Do something about it or wait outside. Nicky wants to go out for dinner." Go out for dinner? "Jeff is making supper, though." She pranced over, hands moving to circle her wife. In a move Rio hadn't seen in centuries, Agatha flinched away. Her beloved wife was definitely not okay.

    "He insists," Agatha told her, voice too high, as she stepped further away. "Something about wanting tamales. Whatever those are."

    Death eyed her wife. Those cracks of hers were starting to show again. Evanora might be gone, but the damage was ever-present. Would be for some time if the young facade was anything to go by. "Yeah...sure. I know a place."

    The restaurant Rio picked was tiny but packed. Couples and small families sat at nearly every table as that horrid crap they called 'Holiday Music' played overhead. Rio would have suggested another spot, but Nicky was dead set on eating tamales for the first time and he didn't want to wait another second. That wasn't the bad part for the cosmic entity, though. No, what upset her was her son's refusal to just let her cook the damn things. She'd suggested it once before they left and a tornado whipped around in the bathroom. Agatha, the unfortunate goddess, had the door slammed on her hand as she brought him his jacket. And instead of apologizing, the boy had stomped a foot and thrown them out of the room. Rio quickly gave in as she healed bruised fingers.

    Truly, Death didn't envy the temper tantrums Agatha had to have faced when he was the ripe age of two. (Seven was shaping up to be just as chaotic, if not worse.) So, to avoid the rest of the house being torn to bits with his new green magic abilities (that he had most definitely inherited from her), Rio and Agatha agreed to endure the torture.

    Her wife, who looked positively scrumptious in a navy-blue turtleneck to match with Rio at the Green Witch's behest, cast a quick spell and forced a group out of the nicest table. She then puppeteered a waiter to seat them immediately. Agatha didn't even have to look in the direction of the mindless mortals as she commandeered the restaurant to her bidding. (Rio had to hand it to the woman, she always knew her way around a mind-control spell.) It was like second nature to the goddess.

    Seated, food ordered, and drinks in shitty plastic cups delivered, Rio turned her attention to her tired-eyed partners. She found herself at a loss for words as the two operated on different levels of 'not okay'. Nicky seemed intent only on eating tamales, to which Agatha shot off another spell to force a cook to try and steam them faster. And Agatha, spells aside, looked very much like she was mentally stuck in Salem. It was all very disheartening for the cosmic entity.

    "Why tamales?" she asked her son, suddenly curious.

    Nicky looked over at her as if she'd asked the most ridiculous question known to man and witchkind. "Mother," he groaned, "you're supposed to eat tamales for Christmas. Duh!" He then returned to his paper menu with a scowl.

    "That's your son," she shot at Agatha.

    Her wife jolted in her seat. "Huh? Oh..." She shifted her gaze over to the coloring child. "Where'd you hear that? Christmas wasn't even celebrated when you were younger." Nicky looked up from his menu, a green crayon in his hand. "From the movies, Mama. That's what families eat." (Interesting. He wasn't using a harsh tone with the goddess.) "Querido, you do know that not everything on tv is real or true, right?" (Damn, they were not doing a very good job of transitioning their son into the modern age.) "A lot of that is pretend and make-believe. Like how the dinosaurs and other paleontological creatures have been gone for some time."

    "Yes, Mother," he huffed. (Definitely Agatha's son.) "But we speak Spanish, so we eat tamales."

    Agatha snorted before falling into a fit of laughter. Her blue eyes twinkled with amusement. "That's called 'stereotyping,' Nicky. Let's not do that."

    Rio grinned widely. She'd forgotten how beautiful her goddess had looked when she was younger. Death was grateful for the reminder. "Mijo, I think the two of us need to have a chat about what you can and can't say about people these days."

    Nicky furrowed his brows. "Why?"

    "Because that could be offensive to someone. And while I know your mama is fine with fighting someone for you, you should still know what's acceptable, and what isn't." She poked his cheek before leaning closer. "You saying that bit about tamales is like saying your mama doesn't know how to season her food, okay?"

    "Hey," Agatha grumbled. "I know how to cook."

    "Yeah, soups," she teased.

    Nicky giggled before having his first real smile in days. "Mama can cook, so I guess that would be lying." He set the crayon down and took a sip of water. "Can we still eat tamales?" "Darling, I'll turn you into a tamale," Agatha laughed. She tickled his side gently. "But I'm starving. Where the hell did our waiter go?" She waved a hand and had the man come running. "Food. Now."

    The waiter bowed before rushing off to collect their entrées.

    "I'm not leaving him a tip," she growled.

    "As if you were going to tip in the first place," Rio laughed. The cosmic entity took the plate of fresh tamales and began to unwrap the steaming rolls of masa for her son. "Careful, Nicky. It'll be very hot."

    Agatha's mini-me seemingly ignored her as he speared a piece and immediately shoved it into his mouth. He chewed widely and dramatically with a, "Ha-zha-zha." His tongue was likely being burnt in the process.

    Rio facepalmed. Why did she bother?

    Agatha, for all her staged prim and properness, picked up a roll with her bare hands. She was quick to drop it back down to her plate with a hiss. "Fuck, that's hot!" "It's almost like I told you it was," Death groaned. "Can one of you listen to me?" "No talk, Mother. Only eat," Nicky told her before forking a second tamale down his gullet. Rio shook her head and ordered a beer.

    "Temple was nice," Tommy told Billy. "I'm a bit bummed Lil' Bro didn't join us...or Ma." "Well, somebody had to talk to Nicky, and it wasn'tgonna be me again." He slid out of the car and closed the door. "There's always tomorrow and the rest of the week." The coven re-entered the house to find Nicky sitting by the fire. Behind him, Agatha was weaving sprigs of mistletoe into his hair. It was a picture-perfect display of domesticity. Not to mention how motherly Agatha seemed in that very moment.

    She had her own wreath of holly and mistletoe resting on her head. Crowned and docile befitting the Divine Mother. She looked something out of a Thomas Kinkade painting. All gentle curls and vibrant colors in the soft orange glow of the fireplace. Rio sat on the sectional, a beer in hand. She had her legs crossed as she watched the two from a safe distance. The Green Witch had yet to change, but she no longer looked as stressed. There was even a small smile on her face that was only interrupted by her infrequent sips. Death shifted her gaze to the group and waved them over.

    "Whoa! Are you gonna stay like that from now on? It's kinda trippy," Alice told the goddess. The goth gave the coven leader a tight grin as she pulled back ever so slightly. "Stay like what?" Agatha asked. She quirked a brow as her hands fell away from Nicky'shair. The ancient witch looked more annoyed than confused.

    "I'm staying with Mama," Nicky told him with a slight scowl.

    Rio gave him a knowing look. "The three of us will join you in a minute." "Sounds good to me." Billy pulled Alice and Jen back. "Hey, maybe don't rush a traumatized woman," he whispered to them once they'd reached the kitchen. "I don't think she even knows she's stuck like that."

    "Yeah, no shit. She wasn't exactly told the nicest things," Jen whispered back. "I'd need more than a banishing circle to get over that trauma. We don't even know if that crazy bitch is gone for good or not."

    "Actually, she's going to talk to me," Rio told them, suddenly in their group huddle. She gave them a manic grin as their heads snapped in her direction.

    "Rio, what the fuck?" Jen hissed. "Do that ghost shit on somebody else. You nearly gave me a heart attack."

    "I couldn't get that lucky," Death lamented. "But you guys can annoy her in the morning. Leave her and Nicky alone for right now. They're working through some stuff together." "Anything we can do to help?" Alice asked.

It was nearly empty, but the teen wondered what number this one was. He could smell the acrid tang of it on her breath.

    Billy snuck a glance at his mother and brother. The two were speaking softly to one another, still in the same spot. "Okay, but do we just not tell her she still looks like a teenage or...?" "If you want her to have another breakdown, sure, Billy. Add to the chaos of the evening." Death threw her empty bottle away and came back with an apologetic expression. "Look, it's been a rough few days for everybody. I would like to take my wife upstairs and make sure she's okay, but Nicky's being stubborn. We don't want to risk another explosive tantrum." The teen gave her a hug. "Hey, we'll get through it. I mean, if Agatha managed to find her way home, then we're not doing too bad, right?" Billy flashed his deathly mother a grin. It grew as Rio begrudgingly (affectionately) returned it.

    "Bye, Mom," Billy told her as she blinked out of existence. "Alright, who wants to help make the dough?"

    Nicholas sat in his mama's lap and played with his stuffed Scratchy. He listened as she hummed to herself, staring into the flames of the fireplace. After several minutes, she began to yawn. Nicholas didn't thing much of it until it felt like she was yawning once every five minutes. Finally, he looked up at her, frowning slightly.

    When had she started crying?

    "Mama?"

    "Yes, Nicky?"

    "Why did you want to celebrate the mortal holidays this year?"

    She looked down at him, nose red. "Because you're here," she told him. It was such a simple admission, and she was rarely so forthcoming. He wondered if she might be feeling sick again.

    Nicholas leaned against her chest with a soft sigh. "If I wasn't sick, would you have celebrated with Mother and I?"

    The goddess chuckled, running a hand through his hair. She was careful not to ruin her hard work. "Oh, I don't know. We would have celebrated Yule in our cabin. Your mother and I would have added on a room for you. You probably would have had several brothers and sisters. It would have been a madhouse of love."

    The child tried to imagine it, but it seemed so utterly impossible. He didn't know what to picture. Would she have had a face like this, but none of the heavy lines of time wearing it down? "Did you really send Mother away after I left with her?"

He turned his gaze back up to watch her face. Nicholas knew she wouldn't lie, but her face usually told him what she wouldn't.

    "She took you from me," the woman sniffed. He could practically hear her heart breaking. Maybe that was why she had all those rules. She was tired of trying to mend something that wouldn't stay fixed. "I didn't even get to say 'good-bye'." Her lips twisted into a heavy frown like a bird nosediving into a cliff.

    "You were sleeping," he reminded her. "But Mother made sure I told you 'bye'. I even gave you a kiss for her." Had she not known? Then again, people didn't know what happened around them while they slept. It only made sense that she never registered his leaving. The hand in his hair stilled. "You what?"

    "I kissed you 'good-bye'," he repeated. "Mother insisted. She was quiet the whole time we walked through the dark, but the boat ride was fun. She let me hold the lantern and row for a little bit, too." Nicholas shrugged. "I wasn't sad until Mother told me I had to wait for you to join us. But then you never came. Every day, I asked. She always told me 'no'." "Food's ready," Mr. Kaplan announced. "Come get it while it's hot."

    Nicholas perked up at the news. "Oh! I want some." He slid out of his mother's grasp and ran towards the table with a grin. "Can I have a plate please?"

    The coven sat around the living room, alcoholic drinks in the hands of the adults, as the children tore into their first round of gifts. Agatha sipped at a glass of water. She had her feet tucked under her as she watched wrapping paper and glitter litter her floor. Thank Her for magic! It would have been a nightmare to clean up.

    "Uh...who's John?" Alice asked as she held up a gift.

But the coven had taken time out of their days to get her something, so she might as well open a few. "We'll start with this small one." It was wrapped poorly, and she could see a bit of the gift itself. Agatha immediately knew it was a gift from her youngest. (That, and he'd wrapped everybody else's gift the same.) She finished removing the wrapper and grinned.

    Agatha huffed. "I gotta stop teaching you guys my tricks. This is getting annoying." "Oh hush. You know you're impressed." Wanda started to shift away.

    Agatha latched onto her, eyes growing wide. "No, don't-" She snapped her mouth shut. She wasn't about to beg the woman to stay for a second time in her life. That was just embarrassing and degrading.

    Wanda shifted back another inch. "Agatha-"

    But then again, the goddess wasn't keen on being alone so soon. Agatha gripped at Wanda's night shirt with more force than she meant to. "Calm down. Rio's not gonna kill you. Just keep your hands where she'll be able to see 'em and you'll be fine."

    The witch laughed. "No, that's not...Your hands are really cold," Wanda explained. "And I'm laying at an odd angle. I just wanted to get comfortable."

    "Oh..." She grew a smirk. "Wanna help me warm them up?"

    Wanda rolled over with a huff. "Goodnight, Agatha."

    The goddess laughed to herself before putting her back against the other witch's. She ran a hand over the cloak, silently bidding her wife a quick, "Please come home, I miss you." The goddess shifted one last time before letting herself sink against the cool, green fabric. It smelled, as it always would, like her wife. Agatha drifted off to asleep and dreamed of flower fields and a deadly, beautiful maiden with an ivory face.

It had mostly been a joke. But a part of the cosmic entity found the goddess' submission endearing. The other part of her couldn't wait to tease the woman.

    She drifted through the crowd, unnoticed. Death stopped in her tracks as a snort managed to rise over the music. There, in the center of the living room, Agatha chatted with a pair of mortals. She looked stunning, as always, but there was a new air around her. The Witch Killer was allowing herself to have a good time.

    Rio pressed forward; heart alight in her chest. Agatha caught sight of her and fucking beamed with an excited grin. Death was seconds away from bending the goddess over to see what a blissfully comfortable Agatha tasted like.

    "John, Sarah," the Divine Mother said. "This is my drop-dead gorgeous wife. Careful, though. We're both jealous sociopaths," Agatha warned honestly. "No, seriously, John. Don't stare at her too long or I'll hex your tongue."

    "We've met before," Death laughed. She slipped an arm around her wife's waist and pressed a kiss to a cool cheek. "Rio," she reminded, a hand extended.

    The couple shook her hand with matching, worried grins. "Is that...Wanda?" Sarah whispered. The two tried to back away, but Agatha glared them into place. Rio turned to find the Scarlet Witch strutting with renewed confidence. The woman had dressed drastically different to anything Death would have imagined on her. There was a Harkness flare about her, clueing Rio in on how Wanda had gotten to that point. Wanda spotted the immortal couple and made her way over with a graceful smile. "Doesn't our resident wallflower clean up nicely?" Agatha purred in her ear. "I'm not fucking Wanda," Rio said with a laugh. "Nice try."

    "I didn't get her all dolled up for you, my love. I did it for her."

    "That's generous of you," Death told her wife. "She looks nice."

    "She better. We worked hard to draw her back out of her shell tonight," Agatha remarked. "Rio!" The witch hugged her tightly before releasing her. "What do you think?" Wanda stuck a hand on her hip and waved the other around.

    "You look formidable," Death informed honestly. "I wasn't aware you were making your debut tonight. Did Agatha force you to?"

    Wanda shook her head with a laugh. "No. I made this decision myself. I wanted to show people the real me." She turned and greeted the mortal couple. "Hello, again. I promise I'm a reformed witch."

    "Fully domesticated," Agatha added. "We've gotta head out, Wandsy. The house will go into lockdown after one, so kick everybody out before then. Stragglers will be sacrificed for Samhain."

    The witch waved them off with a laugh. "Have fun, you two. Don't eat all of Odin's cookies!" Death grinned madly as they stepped out into the backyard. "Ready for your big debut, ¿vida mia?"

    The Divine Mother thumbed a crumb away from the corner of her mouth. "Sure. Whatever. I just wanna see if there's anything worth stealing."

    Valhalla was a sprawling citadel in the sky. Spires of silver and gold towered over the marble paths with Nordic runes and pictographic tales. Odin's castle, a burgeoning stack of ancient bricks, rose to full height the closer the immortal pair got. Silver clouds in a creamy, orange- sherbert sky accented the grey stones and crimson banners.

    In other words, Agatha was already a bit bored of it. That was, until she saw the inside. The Divine Mother and Death were escorted inside where wine, women, and song were in full swing. Dancers pranced around with the smallest amount of clothing to the point Agatha began to wonder if they'd stumbled into a sex club. Seated at the high table, Odin laughed boisterously.

    He was a large man with broad shoulders, white hair, and a bronze eye patch. Odin wore his traditional armor with a crimson cape. He nearly knocked a goblet over as he spoke animatedly. His children sat to his left and right in their own sets of armor and battle regalia. The goddess didn't even try to name the different figures.

    "Do our bosses on high attend parties like these?" Agatha whispered to Rio as they moved closer to the reclining gods. She looked around and searched the crowds for the Ancient Ones.

    "No, but Stevie does go around in my brother's place," Rio told her quietly. She nudged her goddess as they came to a standstill at the foot of the high table. The couple gave a deep bow and shared a grin once their faces were hidden by their hair and hood. "My word!" Odin stood, his thunderous voice echoing clearly across the room. "Agatha Harkness, in the flesh! You're quite the topic, Your Brilliance. What an honor!" He waved away a musician that buzzed in his ear.

    Agatha preened; a hand pressed to her chest. "Stop, you're too kind," she said with an added flourish of her sleeves. "I've brought a gift unlike any other. Homemade sugar and almond cookies made by the Scarlet Witch, herself."

    Rio held the decorative tin aloft, shaking it slightly. "She sends her regards and wishes you and yours a wonderful new year."

    "Lady Death," the man barked before descending into laughter. "You are a sight for sore eyes! Where have you been keeping yourself? We have missed you chilly presence." "You know Eternity. He works me like a dog," the cosmic entity laughed with a roll of her eyes. "I see you're familiar with my wife."

    "Everyone in any realm has heard of the great Witch Killer. She's a legend we tell our young, naughty sorcerers and witches to keep them behaved." Odin laughed heartily before waving them closer. "Thor spoke of a battle that ended with the death of that odious Dr. Strange. If only I had had a front row seat!"

    "That was a fun night. I moved planets," Agatha informed proudly. "Mjolnir isn't all that impressive, either." She shot Thor a look, winking.

    "My son wields it as he sees fit, but he needs more work," the god said in agreement. Odin stroked his beard as he eyed the women. "Don't worry about him. Now, you must partake in the celebration. Go, go."

    Agatha hooked Rio's arm in hers as she led them over to the banquet table. "He's shorter than I imagined," she told her wife. "But he certainly knows how to throw a party. You'd think it was Lupercalia for the Asgardians."

    Death hummed, eyes settling on a woman seated at the high table. "He's mostly harmless. I'm sure he'll bother you about the Council of Godsheads now that you're a permanent deity." She snagged a goblet of mead and sipped at it. "Oh, he's changed it this year." She smacked her lips before humming. "Tastes a bit like blood, if I'm being honest."

    "Father has a bit of pig's blood added to the wine. He believes it will make warriors strong and hearty," a tall, thin man informed the pair. He wore green and gold with a horned helmet. "Lady Death," he greeted.

    Agatha pressed herself closer to the cosmic entity. "And you are?"

    "Loki," the Asgardian informed with an amicable grin. He held his hand out to the goddess. "Ah..." Agatha relaxed. "Asgard's very own bad boy." She accepted the hand and shook it tightly. "Agatha Harkness. Charmed, I'm sure."

    "Very. Thor tells me you're quite the troublemaker." The jokester prince snagged a goblet for himself before pouring it out and filling it with a flask from his pocket. "Anyone willing to take on my older brother is fine in my books. It was rather amusing listening to him whine to my father about how you'd challenged the Avengers. Him and his little club," Loki scoffed. "Well, he was aiding that silly man in kidnapping my coven members," Agatha informed before sipping at the mead. She quirked a brow before taking a large sip. Pig's blood, she found, tasted pretty damn good.

    "Strange had...peculiar methods. Is it any wonder why I chose to stay away from the man?" Loki waved a woman over. "Fetch our guests some party favors. One each, please. Thank you, darling."

    "Still running around playing with Infinity and Eternity?" Rio asked with a laugh. "Last I heard, you managed to get my idiot brother out of his realm to bitch at you." She pressed herself back against Agatha's side as the Asgardian nodded with a grin. "Had a whole mess of affairs thanks to some time-travel issues. But I heard you've gotten into a bit of a sticky situation yourself, Death." He leaned forward with a wink. "The realms caught wind of your taboo union. Eternity was flabbergasted."

    Agatha looked between the two, lips pursed. "I thought you said Eternity rarely left his realm."

    "No, it's true. I like to break in from time to time just to mess with him," Loki told her with a laugh. "His wards are meant for keeping Death and mortals out. And since Death and I are old friends, I do what I can to give him grief."

    "He's the reason for the Bubonic Plague," Rio told her with a wicked grin. "We were trying to see how powerful a single flea was. Loki, here, took an Asgardian mite and let it loose in Europe. My siblings were not pleased."

    Loki shared a look with the cosmic entity. "At the risk of rising your ire, dear friend, I must say it was the first time I'd gotten your hot-headed brother to kiss me. But afterwards, he hid my precious Chitauri Scepter. Wasn't very worth the trouble, in the end." Rio waved him off with a laugh. "Do whatever you want with him. I don't care." Agatha quirked a brow. "Sounds like we need to form a club. The Cosmic Entity Kissers," she said with a laugh. "I can't wait to meet him. I plan on stabbing him at least five-hundred times."

    "Oh, Agatha, you let me know when you do. I've been trying to 'stab' him for quite some time," her fellow god said with a dirty chuckle.

    "I knew I liked you for a reason," she told him. Agatha elbowed him gently with a salacious grin. "It's nice to know the wifey's had a friend to lean on when I was less than friendly." Loki tilted his head; smile dying on his lips. "I'm afraid I don't know what you mean." Rio set her goblet down. "I kept to myself when I wasn't chasing you," she told her wife. "The realms don't know much past my banishment and our unofficial marriage." "Does this have anything to do with the hundreds of incidents the two of you caused?" The man laughed. Loki leaned in, eyes twinkling. "I've heard you two have a very specific way of flirting."

"What was that like? Transitioning from mortal to immortal? I've never heard of it happening before. You must be very special." The imperious god tossed his goblet aside to rip a boar's leg away.

    "Ah, those old biddies," Loki groaned. "Are they still giving you grief, Rio?" The cosmic entity looked down with the slightest of blushes. Agatha grinned to herself. Is somebody embarrassed to have a pet name?

    Rio shot her a look. "When don't they?" she told her friend. Death swished her full goblet and watched the red spill over the rim and down onto the marble below. "Don't be surprised if the realms start falling apart or something. I've crowned Agatha as my queen." The god's eyebrows raised dramatically. Suddenly, his food was a lot less interesting. Loki quickly handed the ham hock off to a stranger. "That's a very serious move, Death! Eternity and Infinity will likely challenge your authority for that." He shifted closer with a fierce look of concern.

The Council will convene the moment the news reaches their realms." Rio rolled her eyes. "In the end, my will is still mightier than theirs. They will have to accept it, or it will be war."

He clasped her shoulder, giving it a tight squeeze. "And, I suppose, a congratulations is in order! May Fate grant you all the mercies, and your victory be swift." If I declared my intent to remove you from the Natural Order, they'd have to leave you alone."

Rio looked at her, a hand gripping at a cool wrist. "By removing you from the Natural Order, you're sort of a rogue god now. But in marrying me, properly this time, you will be as close to a cosmic entity as possible. I would have to half myself, and vice versa, for us to be one. Then, you'd be untouchable by the others."

"So, like, it's marriage taken to a literal sense of 'what's yours is mine'? Like, you'd be Death of all things, and I'd be Life of all things, and vice versa? Right?" Her wife nodded. "But you'd have to live by the rules my siblings and I follow. They're stricter, more...binding."

    "It is a big deal!" Rio pulled away to pace. It was something she didn't do often. Agatha was the pacer of the two. The goddess stopped taking the situation lightly and focused. "You're the first person to ever be elevated this highly. You'll never go back to being an ordinary person. Won't need to eat or sleep or even feel. Like me, you'll outlive everyone. Nicky, Billy, Tommy, Wanda, Alice, Jen...all of them. I would be cursing you with this existence because I can't stand the thought of hurting you."

Personally, I'd rather have to deal with the Universe because I'm gonna handle our bosses one way or another." She turned her wife's face towards her and offered her a warm smile. "And I won't even try to pretend I don't want the power, my love. I've changed, but not by that much. My real concern is your siblings and our bosses. How are they going to react to this news?"

    Death rested her head against Agatha's shoulder. She fiddled with the Fulu protection coin with a sigh. "They'll all probably take time to form a plan. They'll want to convince Eternity and Infinity into stopping me. Once that happens, you might be sent back to Oblivion...forever."

"Guess I better get a move on with my plans then, huh? I'd like to have sex at least once in your palace before turning everything in existence to ash." Death barked out a laugh. She settled her hands on her goddess' waist, pinkies raised. "Glad to know you've got your priorities in check," she said with a content hum. Rio grinned as blue eyes twinkled in the dying light. The moon began to rise directly behind the Divine Mother. Agatha pulled her wife against her roughly before cupping warm cheeks. "Y'know, we're pretty secluded up here. I bet you we could get into some mischief of our own without the others noticing." She kissed along Death's jaw with a smirk as her wife slid a hand under a thigh.

    The house was a nightmarish mass of people and thoughts and sounds. All of Westview seemed to have crammed into the living room in hopes of getting a glimpse of the revived Scarlet Witch. Meanwhile, Billy slipped into the kitchen to give himself a break. His head pounded as he leaned against the kitchen island. He just needed to survive a few more hours and then they'd all be gone......he hoped.

    "Could I trouble you for a glass of water?" a woman asked.

    Billy whipped around to find an older woman with blonde hair standing in the doorway of the kitchen. He furrowed his brows. There was something off about her. Something that made his hair stand on end. She just...stood there.

    "Just a small one," the woman told him. She raised a hand to make a pinching gesture. I know that voice! The Maximoff-Kaplan-Harkness-Vidal stood straight as he faced the intruder. "I don't think you're welcome here."

    The Ancient One laughed softly but her eyes were sharp in the dim light. "I'm not here for a fight, Demiurge. I simply wanted to have a chat." She stayed on the other side of the doorway.

Maybe they'd even take on her sons and keep them safe in her stead. "I've yet to introduce myself," a woman said from her side.

Rio noticed her goddess kept eyeing the armored warriors that passed them.

To step inside her home and speak to one of her coven members! Agatha fumed. Agatha seethed. Agatha knew what to do. "Don't you worry about that. Tonight...Tonight, I make my first move." Agatha raised her gaze to meet Rio's and gave a curt nod. "Remove me from the Sacred Balance."     "That's your thinking face," Wanda said. "Careful there. Agatha says thinking is a dangerous past-time for us Maximoff's."

    "She'd be correct," Jen mumbled.

    "I resent that," Wanda said with a laugh. "It's not my fault I've got such cool, deadly magic." "Maybe not, but you're still known for ripping apart whole superheroes throughout the multiverse," Jen said. "And all because you thought you heard your children calling for you." "The Darkhold was very convincing," Wanda reasoned.

    Wait a minute! "Why don't we find the Darkhold and use it to stop Agatha's bosses?" Billy asked the pair.

    "Do you want to go insane?" Jen said with a laugh. "That book was more than a little dangerous. Agatha terrorized whole continents with that damn thing."

    "True, but she wasn't consumed by it." Wanda raised her mug in a small toast to the woman. "That's the real scary part about everything. She had for the longest and was only slightly more terrifying than she was before."

    Billy looked between the two. "So, if we had one, Agatha could use it and be fine?" "I didn't say that," Wanda was quick to say. "Everything looked fine on the surface, but her memories could only relay so much of what she was experiencing. For all we know, Agatha could have taken a lot of mental damage."

    "No memory surfing," Jen told Billy hastily. "I don't wanna do that again. She had a sad, fucked up childhood. If I ever have to see Evanora again, it'll be too soon." Billy rolled his eyes. "I wasn't gonna-"

    "You guys are so loud," the goddess groaned from somewhere further in the house. "Can you stop using my name for My sake? It's giving me a headache." She shuffled into the kitchen with damn hair and her robe. His divine mother looked like she was hungover or sick with the flu again.

    Billy ignored the others to ask her, "Agatha, would it be a bad idea to use a version of the Darkhold to fight off Infinity and Eternity?"

    The ancient witch was at his side with wide eyes. "Did you find a copy? Let me see. Let me see!" She searched his pockets like a squirrel rooting out an acorn.

    He pushed her away with a scowl. "I don't have one. I was just wondering." "No Darkhold. No dark magic," Wanda told the pair sternly. "Why would you even suggest such a thing to Agatha? That's like offering alcohol to someone who just got sober. That's a terrible thing to do to a person."

    "It was just a question," Billy argued. He slumped back in his chair with a frown. "I'm trying to come up with ideas that can help the coven. Why are we so dead-set on the plan that almost failed?"

    "Hey! It was a good plan," Agatha growled. She pushed Wanda aside to make herself something to drink. (Wanda had hidden all the alcohol at Rio's request.) "I'd argue that it was a great plan, even. I just...forgot Frida was actually a good divination witch." Billy sent her a look. "Agatha..."

    Her eyes widened. "Woof. That sounds like I'm in trouble with you." She cupped her mug of tepid tea with pursed lips. The use of her name after so long seemed to bother her. Billy rolled his eyes. "Not in trouble. I was just going to ask what the hell was going on with that woman."

    Agatha hunched in on herself as she shared a look with Wanda. "Ah...Rio said you were getting the ol' transmission," she said, tapping her temple. Agatha waved him off with a wrinkled nose. "Don't, uh, don't worry about that. 'Kay? I'm a big girl witch. I'm totally okay."

    "Your voice is doing that high-pitched whiny thing, so I know you're lying," Billy huffed. He got up and snagged a tub of ice cream from the freezer. "I get that I'm young, but you guys don't have to hide everything from me." He tugged the silverware drawer open and noisily pulled out a spoon.

    Agatha winced at the sharp, metallic clinks as he slammed the drawer shut. Her face pulled one of her rare, worried looked. All wide eyes and grimacing mouth. He'd only seen it a few times on the Road. Back when she was so concerned she didn't have time for a mask. "I appreciate the help Billy, but the big dogs have it under control," Agatha finally told him. "If you really wanna help, you'll go rest up." She blew on her tea, thinking. "...I sense a storm brewing."

    He stabbed a spoon into the bucket of cookies 'n cream ice cream. "I'll go to bed when I feel like it," he grumbled.

    "He's just worried," Wanda reasoned. "Don't antagonize him."

    "I'm not antagonizing him," Agatha said with a forced laugh. "Look, Billy, there are some things you don't have to know. But if you must know......A bad thing almost happened, but Wanda and Rio swooped in like knights on the lamest horses with you as the banner-bearer. So, good on you or whatever." She stepped closer and rested a hand on his back. "I can promise you that, no matter what, I'll be fine."

    "Now you're just saying that to cheer me up," he told her. Billy ate another spoonful with a glum pout.

    "No, no. I'm being one-hundred-percent serious! See, if things go south, you won't have to worry for too long because I'm taking everybody with me!" She made a series of small fireworks with her magic.

    Jen gulped. "Can't you leave us innocent people out of it?"

    "Oh, where's the fun in that?" Agatha waved the witch off. "We're a coven. We should all burn together. I thought it was about selflessness."

    Billy furrowed his brows. "What if I just imagine the group didn't exist anymore? Would that work?"

    "A little too late in the game," Agatha said, shaking her head. "Maybe if you'd tried that before we got into the thick of it, it could have been done. But with everything as it is, and your lack of control, it's not very likely to work."

    "Well, why didn't you say anything sooner?" he grumbled. Billy ate another spoonful with an incoherent grumble. This was why Agatha always landed in hot water. She never knew when to just ask for help.

    "I didn't know they were a bunch of fake bitches until my first punishment!" Agatha hugged herself. "The second I knew, I started planning. But things got more and more hectic. Dying and trying to keep all of you from dying." She juggled her hands in the air. "Not exactly the best of times to pull you aside and say, 'Hey, Billy, I know you've got some pretty fancy magic, so could you do me a favor and ignore my bosses out of existence? Thanks!' " He practically slammed the tub of ice cream down on the table. "So, your back-up plan was to just keep mentally manipulating me into making you stronger?"

    Agatha threw her hands up with a huff that might have been intended as a laugh. "To be honest, I don't think it's been working, so I've not put much stock in that plan. Stevie, however, seems to think you do have the capability or she wouldn't have come down here to single you out."

    Billy watched his mother as she moved to pace the floor of her kitchen. She had to be formulating a new countermove. That, or she was worried about a new problem. Either way, Billy wasn't looking forward to the coming battle if he couldn't even help with it. "I think I will go to bed," he told his mothers. "Wake me up if you guys can think of something better for me to do."

    Wanda and Agatha fixed him with a forlorn expression. Neither tried to stop him, though, so he didn't bother questioning them further. Then, he heard something he hadn't heard in a while. That's when Billy knew things had to be worse than they were letting on. "Let's get bags packed," Agatha told someone. "Just in case."

    Nicholas looked up as Mama entered his room. He set his book on paleolithic artifacts down next to him. His nose scrunched up as she started rifling through his drawers. "Is it time for a bath?"

    "Oh...no, my love. You might have a sleepover at Teddy's," she told him. Mama gave him a tight smile that usually meant trouble was hot on their heels.

    "Why with Teddy? Did he invite us over?"

    "It's just a possibility," his Mama said. She pulled out one of his larger bags and stuffed it full of his best clothes.

    "Mama...Is everything okay? Mrs. Kaplan wouldn't let me see you when you got home." He tilted his head to the side as the goddess faltered momentarily.

    "Mama got into a fight and I was pretty dirty from it." She gave him another tight grin. "Mother kissed me all better. Don't worry."

    Nicholas slid off his bed to hug her side. "Mother stopped by kiss me goodnight. Are you gonna kiss me goodnight too?"

    The Divine Mother nodded. She set the bag down on the floor before tucking him in. She smoothed his hair down before patting his chest. He smiled up at her, eyes scanning her face for a readable emotion.

    "Y'know, I've known your mother since I was born. Evanora...She didn't want a child, so she gave me away to her. Your mother always watched over me. Kept me safe." Mama stroked his left cheek with a thumb as her voice grew softer. "Now that I know how much she kept me safe, it's time I return the favor. She's got some evil bosses that only Mama knows how to handle."

    "You're gonna be Mother's white knight?"

    "Well, purple knight," Mama corrected with a laugh. The goddess pressed a kiss to his forehead. "Lux vobiscum vade; Amor tecum esto," she whispered.

    He yawned as the spell settled over him. "What's the protection spell for, Mama?" "Can't I just want you to be safe?" Mama asked, eyes twinkling in the light. There was no joy or happiness making them shine. Her eyes were filled with tears.

    "But that's a protection spell for when someone's leaving," Nicholas argued. He gripped at her sleeve. "Are you leaving me again?"

    "No, Nicky. I'm not going anywhere. Mama's just being cautious." She waved for him to scoot over before crawling under the covers to hold him.

    He tested his head on her chest but found only silence. The heart that used to soothe him to sleep lay unbeating beneath his ear. And since they both knew this, Mama began to sing to him. It was a song he wasn't familiar with, but the melody flowed like a winding river. "Oh simple things...where have you gone..."

    Nicholas felt his eyes begin to droop. " 'Night, Mama...Love...you..." The last thing he registered was her hugging him tighter before sleep overtook him.

    One of three children put to sleep, Agatha focused on Tommy next only to turn to the right instead. Before she babied her last son, she stopped by Billy's room to see if he was still awake. The goddess didn't want him to go to bed angry or upset. Not if she could help it. The teen was already snoring, much to her dismay. Agatha entered the room as silently as she could and ghosted a hand over his curls. He leaned into the touch with an incoherent mumble. She gave a sigh, shoulders dropping.

    "This is usually the part where I beg for more time," she whispered. "But there's no one left to beg. It's my mess, not yours, Billy."

    The goddess opened his blinds so moonlight could spill into his room. Let her presence sit with him for the rest of the night. She kissed his temple before whispering, "Lux vobiscum vade; Amor tecum esto."

    Nicky, her intuitive little boy, had guessed correctly. It was a protection spell for travelers. Only they would be traveling, and her staying. She didn't know where or when the Ancient Ones would strike next, but they had to know what she was up to now. Her children would be their next targets.

    Agatha stood from her spot and moved to pack the teen a bag. Her foot hit a solid lump. She nearly laughed as she looked down to find a small duffle bag. Billy must have heard her or read her thoughts. She eyed the room before pulling the door shut behind her. Tommy, thankfully, was still awake. He sat at his desk, sketching. His pencil scratched at the paper as he concentrated on trying to draw a shark. Agatha grinned widely. "I'll give you five bucks if you draw a bimbo getting eaten," she told him as she leaned over his shoulder.

    Her son laughed darkly. "I was actually gonna have the shark eating that guy you killed." "Oh, Strange? That asshole?"

    Tommy nodded. "Yeah, his ass fuckin' sucked Ma Squared." He set his pencil down to lean back in his desk chair. "What're you doin', Ma? Final rounds?"

    Agatha leaned back against the wall with crossed arms. "Something like that. Thought I'd check in on you. Anything you wanna tell me?"

    The speedster tapped his chin in thought with pursed lips. "I know you gotta be worried if Billy's worried. He told me to pack a bag, and I've never had to do that before." "Well, he's got some bad memories associated with that request." Agatha pushed off the wall and gestured to the bed. "Back when I was a skeleton, we found out Wanda was out and about. I knew she'd go after Billy first, but I wasn't sure if she'd kill me or not." Tommy laughed as he crawled under the covers. "Sounds about right."

    "Yeah, tell me about it," she grumbled. "But Billy screwed up my soul so bad that no matter what happened, I was going to die a true death. So, I sent the coven off so Rio and I could face Wanda together. Then I thought better of it and sent Rio off too." "And then you died?" Tommy asked her as he snuggled against his pillows. "Wanda's always been one for dramatics. She crushed my bones to bits. Left me in pieces for Rio to find. I think I was dead for six or seven hours, but boy did that shit drag," Agatha said with a laugh. "That's why Billy's worried. He thinks all of that is going to happen again." "Except you can't die this time," Tommy reiterated.

    Agatha settled her hand on his head. "No, I can't die. But it looks like war is coming. A war that could decimate everything in existence if we're not very careful. I'd tell Billy that, but then he'd focus on all the negative bits and be angry with me. Then I'd really have to worry about my chances."

    "Bro just loves the fuck outta ya, Ma. I'd still tell him. Don't piss him off before the end of the world," Tommy told her. "I know you've got a different way of showin' your love, but don't hide shit from him. It seems to really bug him."

    "Look at you," she praised gently. "Now I know who inherited the braincell." Agatha tapped his nose before tucking him in more securely. "Love's a funny thing when I'm concerned. I'm still learning how to go about it without screwing things up."

    "With a Ma like yours, I'm not surprised. She was a bitch, Ma. Like...Jesus fuckin' Christ!" Agatha laughed, nodding. "Yes, I'm aware. She was a nightmare."

    "I'm glad you and Ma and Ma Cubed are so much better than her. And I think that if you just trusted your instincts a bit more, you'd find it a bit easier to let yourself love." Tommy rolled over onto his side, undoing all her neat, hard work. " 'Cause we love ya, Ma. You're my favorite serial killer."

    Agatha blinked back tears as she pushed his shaggy hair out of his face. "I love you guys, too," she whispered. And as scary as it was to say aloud, it was true. "Get some rest, Tommy. We'll hold down the fort."

    " 'Kay, Ma. Kick some ass for me," he mumbled as he shut his eyes.

    "All the ass will be kicked," she said softly. Agatha pressed a kiss to his temple the way she'd done with his brothers. "Lux vobiscum vade; Amor tecum esto."

    She watched as her magic settled over him with a soft glow of white. If they had to leave her sight, they would be safe. Agatha had to believe it as much as Billy had to believe in her and Rio. She got up and switched off his lamp. Moonlight taking her spot by the bed, Agatha pulled the door shut.

    "Teddy's all set should we need to evacuate the kids," Rebecca told Agatha the second she reached the last step. "His parents have an RV trailer we can stay in until things have blown over."

    "Good. I want every single ward you can think of put up around their property. I don't want any of them getting hurt on our account. The neighbors, I couldn't care less." Agatha habitually poured herself a glass of wine. "Any sign of our friends, Wanda?" "Nothing. Bodies are still secure," the former soldier reported. "Rio took Frida's soul before leaving for work."

    "Gonna form a whole new gauntlet with those five," Agatha muttered into her glass. She took a sip before her setting her glass aside. "Well, what do we think? Are we gonna come out victorious or not?"

    "I believe in you," Alice told her. "Even dying hasn't stopped you from getting what you want."

    "And, boy, have you died," Jen added. "How many times is that now?"

    "If you consider my banishment a kill, five. If not, four now. Out of all of them, being stabbed with our marital dagger was the least fun." She made a face at the reminder and reached for her glass again.

    "See, you've got this," Jeff told her. "That's way more times than any of us mere mortals." Agatha pursed her lips. She didn't remember getting chummy with the man, but he'd never made it a point to be her enemy after Rio revealed her true nature all those months ago. She nodded jerkily at his comment before downing the rest of the wine.

    "I want all bags loaded and ready to go first thing in the morning. Regardless of what happens, you're all going to leave for Teddy's around lunch time. Rio and I will focus on keeping up pretenses until those biddies make their first move," she told the coven. "I'm staying," Wanda argued. "You know it would be good to have me as back-up." "You don't think the kids will want you?"

    "I can astral project and be with both of you," she argued.

    Agatha scowled. "Love that you can do that, but it should be something I can do." "Surely you can now. You're a fully-fledged goddess of magic," Alice reasoned. "Probably. But I don't have time to find out." She frowned. "I shouldn’t have wasted so much time on the basics. You're all weak, helpless kittens without me."

    Her coven laughed gently as they looked to her. It was a disheartening thought, her lack of preparation. If anything happened to any one of them, it was on her. As their coven leader, as the oldest, as the Divine Mother, she would be failing them.

    "Everything's going to be okay," Wanda soothed. "You've got this. You're Agatha Harkness, Divine Mother." She moved to rub circles into the goddess' back. "Your coven has faith in you."

    "Yeah. The Road couldn't kill us," Jen said with a wide grin. "No offence, Ali." The goth held up her hands. "Road didn't kill me, Agatha did," she mumbled. That earned a small laugh from the ancient witch. "You guys drive me up the damn wall most days. I want to punch one of you at least twice in one hour. My kids have a way of tearing the house up. But fuck if I don't love it that way."

    "You are my sisters...and brothers...in the craft. We're missing two, but they're with us. Actually, Rio's probably outside kicking Frida in the face, and Lilia's resting somewhere laughing at the sappy dramatics I'm putting everyone through."

    Agatha toasted her empty glass to the coven, all of them. The weight of the world, the Universe, Life and Death, rested on her shoulders. She needed to have her head in the game if she were to protect this small corner of peace she'd found. If she wanted Nicky to grow up to be the witch he was meant to be. If Jen and Alice were to marry and love each other until the end of their days.

    She had to be ready now. She had no other choice.

    Outside, the moon rose higher in the sky. In the astral plane, Stevie met with Eternity. Their silhouettes danced against the haze of his barrier as shouting ensued. The heavens began to quake.

    Chapter End Notes I'd like to think Agatha's such a worrier. Doesn't matter if it's a coven of two or ten. That woman's going to want her loved one's safe and sound and well away from whatever trouble she might have called to her.

    I think a good example is the way she moves to protect them when Rio is summoned to the Road. I think about that a lot.

    What Fresh Horrors Await Us? (It's the Big Finish, After All)
