"We just renovated it, you bitch!" She shot a bolt of lightning at the witch. It redirected and struck Agatha instead. The goddess slammed into the hex's wall with a shout. "These rules are so annoying," she grumbled once she finished smoking. "Wanda, get my wife out of that damn bubble. I want to drain the blood from Betty's body with our dagger."

    As Wanda flew off, Stevie and Eartha teamed up against her. Agatha hissed as they formed whips and tried to subdue her. Keen on sending the three straight to hell, Agatha tapped into the magic she'd inherited from Rio. It made her fingers tingle as she commanded the earth to assist her. The uprooted tree came to life and wrapped its roots around Betty as she tried to kill Wanda. The witch struggled against it as it absorbed her. As the last of the bark closed up over her, Agatha made a fist, imploding the tree.

    Stevie bore down on her with a trident of light with a shout. The middle prong caught her chest before Agatha's grip stopped it from going deeper. The Ancient One exerted more force, digging the outer prongs into her skin. "I'm going to roast your spawn the way you roasted Evanora. You're going to watch as I destroy everything you love, Agatha. And just when you think I've finished, I'll have Lady Death imprisoned for her treason." "Careful, Stevie. The last person who threatened me with a good time wound up mulch," Agatha warned with a grunt. She fought to keep the trident from impaling her further, but she could see Eartha coming towards her from the side. Agatha sucked in a breath before slipping into the Ether. Her body rippled with the change before she popped back out with a wheeze. Her skin crawled at the sensation.

    "Oh, Agatha, you don't even know what you're doing," Eartha taunted. "You're going to shred that body."

    "Let her," Stevie laughed. "Agatha's always been her greatest enemy." She hefted her trident at Agatha.

    Agatha hissed as it caught her arm and tore at her with a burning sensation. "Any day now, Wanda," Agatha growled. "Should've taken one of Tommy's knives." She formed three large, swirling balls of black and purple balls of magic and fired them off like cannon balls. Her magic fizzled out before it could reach them. Agatha growled loudly in frustration. Stevie snapped, freezing her in place. "You forget who holds your leash." Agatha braced herself as she dropped from the sky. She landed next to her grave with a thud. The goddess fought against the magic. Getting real sick and tired of this shit. Agatha glared daggers at the women as they hovered overhead like vultures.

    The witches lowered themselves until they flanked her sides. Eartha threw up a spell, splitting the yard in half. Wanda stopped trying to free Rio to get back to Agatha. Her attempts were nigh feeble, but not without a small percentage of getting through. Already, Eartha's barrier shook and splintered as the Scarlet Witch beat at it with her fists. Stevie raised Agatha back up to have her stand in front of her.

    Then, the bitch flicked her into the forcefield. Agatha couldn't even cry out as a pressure held her in place. Her body protested the invisible weight to the point of making her nauseous. "Leave her alone," Wanda shouted. She beat at the forcefield with all the magic she could muster. The second it vanished, Eartha just put up a new one with a laugh. Stevie squished her cheeks as she came to gloat in Agatha's face. "Don't you remember what I told you? If you had just done your job, I would have left you alone." The Ancient One took the middle prong of her trident and pressed it over where she would have had a heart. "There are some things even I can't overlook, Agatha. The destruction of a soul and your unauthorized union with Death." She tutted. "Bring me Death," she ordered her subordinate. No! Agatha tugged and tugged at the magic as she watched them drag Rio through the forcefields. Wanda! Please!

    "If we pass your punishments to Agatha, you behave. Let's see if it's true in reverse," Stevie announced. She removed her trident from Agatha's chest and pointed it at Rio's head. "It's not exactly the Solstice, but I have authorization to give you a time out. I'm thinking one month. It worked so well for Agatha."

    Agatha felt the last thread of her sanity snap right as Wanda broke the forcefield.

    Rio felt the air shift as Agatha's eyes began to glow white hot. There was a tug at her being as the goddess drew on her strength and magic to overpower the magic holding her in place. Death fell to her hands and knees as her form began to tremble. She'd happily give her power to Agatha, but...maybe not like this. Not when Agatha was so new to accessing the powers of the Universe.

    Wanda moved away from Agatha and landed at her side. "Hey, not to complain or anything, but I'm pretty sure she's draining me of my magic," the witch panted. "I'm, like, really tired." "We have to..." Fuck! Death had never ached before, but she was now. It felt like she was being wrung dry like a sponge. "We have to snap her out of it."

    The goddess, however, was far too gone to be reasoned with. Gone was Agatha Harkness. Now, the Ancient Ones faced the Wrath of the Universe. A being with only one mission on her mind.

    The ground heaved upwards before fire burst to life in its place. Rio grabbed Wanda and moved them upwards. The sky within the hex swirled with clouds until it was a lovely shade of black. The Ancient Ones held their ground as they faced Cosmic Agatha. Her wife had grown to be about twenty-feet tall and cloaked in the stars.

    "I'm all for her destroying them, but maybe not along with us," Wanda squeaked. "I don't know if my hex can hold all of this in. My head's starting to hurt from everything," she said as she pressed a hand to her forehead.

    "You better keep that wishful thinking up, then. It's the only thing keeping her from killing everyone within a five-mile radius. Maybe more," Death informed. She clutched at her side as more of her was absorbed into Agatha. "We're in the same boat, unfortunately. If she takes all of me, you just focus on keeping her contained until she burns herself out." Wanda looked panicked. "Don't leave me! This wasn't in any of her lessons. How do I hold a cosmic entity off?"

    Death wheezed out a laugh as her flesh melted away. "Well, it's that or a whole lot of dying for the town. Personally, I don't care, but you might."

    The Scarlet Witch closed her eyes and relaxed her body. "Okay...........Okay, I can do this." Rio felt a tingle as Agatha's syphoning slowed to a stop. "Was that you?" Wanda nodded as she kept her eyes shut. "Think you can work like that? I can't..." She winced. "Can't do much more than hold her back."

    "You're doing great, Maximoff. Keep it up. I'll see what I can do to stop the apocalypse." Death floated away and placed a hand on Agatha's shoulder. "Sweetheart...Gonna need you to reel it in a bit," she told her wife. "You're doing a number on Wanda and I." Cosmic Agatha, beautiful as she was, brushed the hand away with a growl. Agatha formed a spear of magic and hurtled it at the trio. They spread out before it collided with the ground with a deafening BOOM!

    "It's giving King Kong if I'm being honest," Rio told her. She ducked as Stevie hefted a trident at the giant. "Oy! Watch it," she growled. "Fuck it. Kill the bitches. Then, I'll talk you out of your rampage." Death laughed as Stevie threw her an ugly look.

    Her wife fired off lasers from her eyes before a line of trees imploded. Wooden splinters poofed like confetti. Death shivered. Her wife was so fucking hot when she was tearing shit up.

    Stevie and Eartha combined their magic to create a larger trident. They threw it at Cosmic Agatha who caught it like a toy. She absorbed the magic before swiping at them with her other hand. The air sizzled with her magic as it sucked the life from nearly everything around her. Stevie condensed a ball of light between her palms before pushing it out towards Agatha. "Move," Death told her wife.

    Agatha couldn't hear her in her bloodlust. It struck her square in the chest and sent her stumbling backwards. Rio reached for her knife, ready to skin the Ancient One. She felt her would-be stomach drop as her hand closed around empty air.

    "Where did it...?" She turned back to Agatha as she formed a pole of deep, deep violet magic. It looked black before something shiny glinted on the end. Death nearly dissipated on the spot. "You turned my dagger into a scythe?" (Oh, she was so going to get on her knees and worship her wife if they managed to survive everything.)

    "Don't distract me," Wanda hissed. (She still stole a peek and had to agree with Rio. Agatha as a giant, glowing reaper was a bit of a turn on.)

    Cosmic Agatha swung her scythe and cut through the hex and Eartha. Wanda gave a cry as her hands flew to the sides of her head. The Ancient One was absorbed into the blade and hardened into a stone along the pole near Agatha's hands. Stevie raised her chin as Agatha pulled back for another blow.

    "I have already summoned the cavalry, Agatha. Even if you render me useless, Eternity won't stand for this." The remaining Ancient One prepared one final blast.

    Rio grit her teeth. She had no magic she could use to stop Stevie. And even if she used her form to block the spell, there wasn't enough of 'her' to shield Agatha. She could only hope Agatha would be okay after such a blow. Death flew to Wanda and curled around her as Stevie used all magic available to attack Agatha as the scythe swung to meet it. There was a bright light before the equivalent of a small bomb went off in the hex. The air rumbled and grew hot before the blast whooshed towards the boundaries of the hex. Thanks to Rio's quick thinking, Wanda was able to maintain her hex and keep everyone on the other side from a horrible, fiery fate. However, the force of it did render the woman unconscious. The hex folded in on itself as the spell faded away. Death lowered them to the ground that was, thankfully, no longer aflame. It was, however, horribly torn up and churned into a mess of dirt, roots, and house debris.

    Agatha stumbled forward with a groan; a hand pressed to her head. "Did we win?" Death laughed. "I don't see Stevie, anymore. I'd like to think we did." She winced as her stolen essence rushed back to her. Rio swayed before letting out a breath. She dropped Wanda to catch Agatha as she sunk to her knees. "Easy there. Easy," she soothed. "Just took a bit too much and used too much. It's okay."

    Her wife twitched. "Am I dying?"

    "No, my love, but your form is very badly damaged." Rio pressed a kiss to her cheek. "Just give me a moment to reconnect with the Earth and I'll heal what I can." "I wouldn't bother," a voice boomed.

    Death growled as she raised her gaze to lock eyes with her new guest. "Five minutes." "You're no one to make demands of me, Death," Eternity told her. He landed quietly and looked around at the damage. "This is why we agreed to never mingle with the mortals. They're so temperamental."

    "Yeah...Well, she's not a mortal anymore, so it doesn't count," Rio argued. She settled Agatha next to Wanda. The red head stirred with fluttering eyes and a gasp.

    "Oh man...I was really hoping we'd get a break before moving on to them," Wanda huffed as she sat up with a groan.

    Death couldn't agree more.

    Agatha lay on the ground as she tried to regain feeling in her limbs. Had she gone a bit overboard with the whole 'Cosmic Wrath Titan' bit? Perhaps. But Stevie had asked for it! She was just doing what any good wife with access to the powers of the Universe would have done. Then again, she couldn't remember much of what she'd done, so it hadn't been all that worth it. Maybe Rio would give her a play-by-play.

    Someone was talking. Or multiple someones, actually. Their words were a jumbled mess of heated sounds. The sound waves thundered against her skull (did she have a skull?) and made her migraine worse. Agatha didn't really care to find out who or what they were saying. All Agatha knew was that Eternity had finally made his appearance, and that gave her enough strength to turn her head and size her next opponent up.

    The man bore a striking resemblance to Infinity facial feature-wise. Eternity had solid black eyes and a muscular build for a man who sat around all day and night. His hair, however, was silver with age. And unlike his sister, who had an excellent sense of fashion, Eternity was shirtless with a skirt of hide the likes not seen since the dawn of man. Brass bands wrapped around his wrists and biceps as he argued with his sister.

    Overall, Agatha found him lacking.

    "Why'd he go quiet?" she heard Wanda ask.

    "He's summoning Infinity," Rio explained. "We're about to-"

    Westview vanished as they were pulled elsewhere. Wanda, unfortunately, was left in Westview. Agatha sat up as a fraction of her pain dissipated. Her wounds, unfortunately, were still present and bleeding freely. She wasn't too pleased to find herself hurt, either. I thought I was an unkillable god now, she thought to herself bitterly. Agatha rose to find an endless sea of white and immediately grew irritated.

    Agatha groaned, stomping a foot. "No...Not this place. This place sucks! If we're to decide the fate of all living things, we should do it surrounded by all living things." Wasn't her struggle worth something to the Universe? Couldn't they pick a beach to fight on? There was a pop that pulled hers and Death's attention.

    "Hello again," Infinity greeted. She waved a hand at Agatha with a dazzling grin. Her golden toga shimmered against the white in harsh manner.

    Agatha did not find Infinity amusing. She found her rather annoying. Wasn't it customary to give the opposition at least a fortnight to prepare for the next battle? Rio scowled as she pulled her wife closer. She'd slipped into her cosmic form. Her crown was even on display. Agatha guessed it was time to get serious. (Meeting your partner's family was always so stressful.) "Trying to steal my wife?" Death growled as she eyed her sister. "Oh, heavens, no!" The cosmic entity pressed a hand to her chest with a laugh. "After all the work I put in to have you two finally tie the knot? Don't be so silly." Infinity moved to sit as a chair materialized from nothing. She crossed her legs right as Loki appeared. "It wasn't easy convincing those odious women to give her a chance, either."

    Agatha looked at the group with pursed lips. "...I'm lost again. Somebody catch me up." "Not sure, but I think we're about to negotiate a deal," Loki whispered. "It seems war is the last thing these three want."

    "Well, yeah! We just wanted a break from all the bullshit micromanagement Dickhead here instilled." Agatha jutted her thumb at the scowling man that sat to Infinity's left. "In fact, just give me five minutes with him and I'll be good. A quick foot up the ass ought to straighten him out."

    Loki shook his head quickly. "Just take a seat, my friend. Let's not escalate things further until we have a better grasp on the situation. If an agreement can't be reached, then we'll take turns." He shot Eternity a sly grin before winking.

    "Maybe start explaining what the hell is going on," Rio suggested, eyes boring into her sister's face. She sat, a chair appearing for her as well.

    "I don't trust this place to give me what I want so I'm gonna..." Agatha sat on the armrest and threw her arm over the back of her wife's would-be throne. "Okay. All set." "It might be best to start with Infinity," Loki reasoned. "You seem the most forthcoming." He gestured to her to start whenever she wanted to. Agatha wanted to gag at the intervention- styled set up.

    "I don't mind sharing," Infinity said with a pleasant smile. She gestured gracefully to her brother. "After I came to terms with what we'd done, I found a soul and tossed it into your corner of the cosmos," she told Death. "I might have tweaked it a bit to catch your eye, but Agatha Harkness is authentically herself in case you were worried about the morality of my actions." She grinned at Agatha. "And the fact you two grew to love each other was just an added bonus. After the Demiurge tampered with her, I negotiated her ascension to godhood." Infinity tilted her head in a way similar to Rio when she was imparting some juicy secret. "I'm quite pleased with how everything sorted itself out. I was not aware, however, that Eternity had the Ancient Ones punish the both of you with such barbaric methods. I was never consulted for those decisions. I'd like to extend my apologies. I should have kept a closer eye on those five after they failed to make her a full god."

    Eternity scoffed. "You would apologize to a mortal? Sister," he said with a rumbled groan as he shook his head disapprovingly. "Death was being appropriately punished for going against my better judgement." Eternity sniffed as his face screwed up into a scowl. "And you said the mortal would be a pet, not a lover."

    Death fixed her brother with a glare. "Hold your tongue when you think such rotten things about my wife."

    "Now let's not-" Loki made a face as the siblings leaned in towards each other with rippling energy.

    "I do not recognize your union just like I don't recognize you," Eternity boomed. "You will tuck that thing away or resign yourself to the same fate."

    "If I go, the rest of the Universe goes with me. There will be no flow of anything without me," Death said with a harsh laugh. "So, think real hard about what you want to happen here."

    "You're being unreasonable, Eternity," Infinity added. "We have punished her enough. More, even." She held up a hand to keep Death in her seat. "You'll have to face your issues at some point and learn to forgive."

    Agatha looked between the three with pursed lips. "What if we just kick his ass? Just a thought, you guys." She raised her left hand in a half-hearted shrug.

    "Stay out of this, mutt" Eternity growled. "You've tainted this realm and my sister long enough."

    "Oh, you do not want to mess with me, fucker!" Agatha was on her feet with a fist of purple. "Let me show you what this mutt is made of!" She swung on him with a satisfying crack of her fist. The goddess barked out a laugh as he tumbled out of his seat with a choked gasp. Eternity growled before throwing himself at her. He slammed her back into the ground with as much force as he could muster. The back of her head bounced, but she didn't give the blow any time to register with whatever she had for pain receptors. Eternity snarled as she sneered up at him.

    "I've fought children stronger than you," she laughed. Agatha headbutted him, then kneed him, before finally used her magic to deliver a swift blow to his side. She raised herself as Infinity and Death stood to try and calm the two. She ignored them. "Come on. You wanted to act all tough with your high-horsed, ass-backwards coping methods. I'm more than ready to put you in the ground."

    Eternity grabbed his chair and swung it like a bat. Agatha was knocked over with a shout. "I'll make you regret your existence." He sent her flying with a surge of cosmic energy. "Agatha!" Death unsheathed her dagger and raced to stab him. She growled as Infinity stopped her. "What are you doing? I thought you were on my side."

    "I am," Infinity told her. "But if the three of us fight, there will be disastrous consequences." "She is half of me," Death reminded. "There are already consequences." She freed her arm and lunged for Eternity.

    Agatha appeared from above with a spear of magic. She dug it deep into Eternity's back with a laugh. "No, no, honey. This one's mine." Agatha removed the spear before twirling away from a stream of golden magic. "Missed me~," she sang.

    Eternity growled. "I'll burn your world to ash," he vowed.

    "Don't make that threat so lightly," Agatha hissed. "I'm all for going three for three on making people eat those words." She blasted him with arch of magic. It didn't move the cosmic entity. Eternity pushed his sisters and Loki away with a gust of gold magic as he ran at Agatha. He swung at her, forcing her backwards. Agatha blocked a kick which allowed him to land a blow on her cheek. She fell to the floor and rolled over quickly before he could catch her in the back with another attack. Eternity produced a sword with his magic and swung it down. Loki intercepted it with his scepter. "Can we please try to contain ourselves?! The realms will shatter if any more of this fighting continues." The god forced the blade up and away from Agatha.

    She wiped at her busted lip with a hum. "Ah, I almost had him," she told Loki. "Give me five more minutes. I promise to leave you a little something when I'm done." Death pulled Agatha away from her brother. "If you so much as lay a hand on my family...," she growled. The sound rumbled deep within the astral plane like an earthquake. (There was an earthquake, actually, as well as several sudden storms thanks to their scuffle, but they wouldn't find that out until much later.)

    The goddess tossed her hair back as Rio held her up. Now that she wasn't in the heat of fighting, her head didn't feel too good. She changed tactics. "You're not the only one who's lost a son," Agatha told Eternity. "You ever thought about that?"

    "You lost a mortal child that would have been dust regardless," he snapped. "You could not possibly comprehend the depths of my sorrow." Eternity puffed his chest up as he looked down on her.

    "She would," Infinity said in Agatha's defense. "If you pulled your head out of the sand for more than a few seconds, you would know. They both lost their son, too, Eternity. And it wasn't easy dealing with their grief while Agatha was being hunted."

    "I know about their trials," Eternity sneered. "I set them into motion." Agatha formed a new spear. "Yeah, I'm gonna kick his ass until one of us stops moving." "We don't know the full story just yet," Loki reasoned. At least he had the decency to look stressed about the situation. He looked torn to be the third-party representative. "Why should we listen to him? The fucker clearly doesn't care. He's fucking smiling about what we went through," the goddess spat. She readied her spear to stab him in the chest. "I will happily tell you," Eternity told the group with a laugh. It was a harsh sound. Agatha knew it well. "I made sure neither of you would ever be happy. You don't deserve it. Not after what you did to Entropy." He stepped forward, black eyes boring into the goddess. His lips twisted into an impish grin that made her stomach twist. "I ordered your punishment to be an unspecified amount of time in Oblivion. If not for Death's unruliness, you would have rotted there. You are nothing to this trio, and I will ensure you return to nothingness." Agatha kicked his shin, then kneed his face as he doubled over.

    "You cantankerous..." Eternity hissed.

    "I'm shaking in my boots," she taunted, voice whiny. Agatha flipped her hair with a scoff. "Unfortunately for you, I happen to have a pretty kick-ass coven. And they made damn sure none of your stupid little mind tricks worked for very long. So maybe shut the fuck up and listen for once." Agatha huffed, fanning herself. "Why the fuck is it so hot up here?" Infinity and Death peered at her curiously.

    "Maybe sit down," Loki suggested. "You've used a lot of magic."

    Rio helped her over to a chair with a hand against Agatha's chest. "You cannot nullify our marriage," she reminded Eternity. "You can stop trying."

    "Why should I let you have everything I couldn't?" Eternity growled. If Agatha knew any better, she'd say he sounded like he wanted to cry. "You took him from me!" "We," Infinity said, gesturing between her and him, "we killed Entropy, Eternity. It was no fault of Death's. You know it to be true, yet you still entertain this notion that it was her doing. Can you, for once, accept your part in his passing?"

    Agatha leaned back as her vision began to swim. "Yeah, what she said!" She pointed to her sister-in-law before giving the woman a thumbs-up. "Hey, quick question. Do you guys get dizzy?"

    Rio pointed her dagger at Eternity. "S S ST T TO O OP P P!"

    "Don't raise your voice at me," Eternity huffed. "I will do as I please." He pointed the tip of his sword at where Agatha's heart would have laid. "I know where your heart lies, Death." Agatha smirked up at him despite her discomfort. "Hey, you wanna go for round two, you won't get any complaints from me. In fact..." She lurched forward, grasping his sword to keep it from piercing her, and pushed him back towards the ground. It took some effort on her part, but Agatha managed to open a portal to a random realm and let them tumble downwards. "...I think you and I are overdue for a chat."

    "AGATHA!"

    The pair landed on a sandy shore that looked straight out of Billy's first trial. Agatha prayed to Herself that they weren't on the Road again. There was only so much she could take, and the Road was not on that list. But wherever they were seemed to be secluded. It was perfect. Agatha closed the portal up with a grin. "That ought to do the trick." She twisted the sword out of his grasp and flung it away from them. It landed with a sa-kish in the sand. Agatha eyed the water and decided against using it to heal her bleeding arm. Salt water didn't sound very soothing.

    The goddess sniffed before looking down at her brother-in-law with a wrinkled nose. "Alright, Buster, it's just you and me now. So, let's get one thing straight before I lay into you. My name is Agatha Harkness, and I'll kick your ass if you use anything else." The goddess fixed her hair with a huff. There were going to be so many tangles to brush out later. "You lost your son, Entropy, in a fight. I didn't. That's one argument I know I won't win. I was lucky Nicky got to go in his sleep. He was warm. He was safe. He was loved." Agatha swallowed past a lump as Eternity glared up at her. "I'm going to tell you something you needed to have heard, but it didn't exist those days." She schooled her face, letting her anger dissipate. "I'm sorry for your loss. I'd tell you it gets better, but it doesn't. It sucks. You feel like there's a gaping whole inside of you all the time, and nothing will ever fill it." "Your child was insignificant and-"

    "Wrong!" Agatha shook her head and forced herself to even her tone. She planted her hands on her hips as she got her emotions under control. "Nicky meant something to Death and I just like Entropy meant something to you and your family. No, people don't grieve the same. No, I don't know your specific kind of pain. I do, however, have an inkling. So, if you would be so kind, allow me to put my coven's teachings to use and offer my support." She shifted her weight with a soft, "You can talk to me. I'll listen."

    Eternity stared up at her with a jaw that visibly clenched and unclenched. "...My son was a troublemaker. He liked to annoy Infinity with his childish antics. Death...she liked to tell him stories of the life that grew on planets around the universe." His tense shoulders lowered an inch. Eternity looked away from her to stare at the water that lapped at the sand feet away from them.

    Agatha nodded and dropped to the sandy ground in silence. "Go on," she whispered. The cosmic entity bowed his head as he pressed a hand into the sand. "One of our first planets, one like Earth, Entropy tended to bombard with storms of the astronomical variety. One day, he sent a volley of asteroids. It ruined all of our progress. Infinity had reached the end of her patience. She turned on him. The battle was ruthless."

    The goddess blinked as images flooded her mind. She gripped at her robes as the pounding in her head grew.

    There was a young boy with a devious smirk hiding behind pillars. He had curls like his father and aunt, but his eyes were crystal blue. His giggles echoed throughout the universe as he played a never-ending game with his aunt. A beautiful planet came into view. Lush grasses and towering trees shook with the wind of a storm. Then, there was fire and ash and Infinity. She was larger than life with a hand reaching for the boy. Eternity appeared, shouting wordlessly at the two. Gold and cerulean magic clashed. The planet exploded. The two cosmic entities teamed up against Entropy.

    "Death came to his aid, but we had massacred him by then. She was silent and above our fighting. It was the first time she had ever reaped a soul. Death...she has always cared about the Cycle of All Things. It was with his death that she chose to ferry souls personally." Rio in her original form of black robes, sans her crown but not any less regal, stood over the broken form of Entropy. Fire and blood painted the scene. Death knelt and took Entropy's hand, but he did not stir. Eternity shouted at her. Tried to wave her away. Death stood and pulled at the soul, not the body. Entropy came away with her, hand in hand. Eternity tried to chase after them, but Death's realm was a special place they could not enter. Not yet. "When she came back, Infinity and I cast her out of the astral plane. We condemned her to roam the planets she coveted so much. Death chose to predominately settle on Earth. She took her punishment with little argument. Death only asked that we visit her." Eternity dropped his head into his hands. "I never did. Instead, I turned her away. I have never spoken a word to her since that day. Not until Stevie came and announced what you two had done. I could not hold my tongue another moment longer."

    Agatha sighed. "Yeah...That's not...It's not the best way to handle your grief. I would know." She let out a breath as the memory onslaught finished. "We're two peas in the same pod, you and I." Agatha rubbed at her chest and frowned. Must have lost Alice's coin when those idiots attacked me... "After Nicky, I pushed R- Death away. I blamed her, too. It took a pretty long time to come to terms with everything."

    "But you have your son back. I will never see mine again," Eternity growled, but he'd lost his edge. His form even seemed to sag as if he were melting. It seemed the cosmic entity was unfamiliar with holding humanoid forms.

    She pursed her lips as she eyed him carefully. "That was just a happy accident. I never expected Nicky to be able to return. Not really." Agatha stood up. The need for a bed was growing. "Come on. Let's stretch those chicken legs of yours. You can tell me more about Entropy if you want......It helps."

    Her brother-in-law followed after her. His bare feet slipped and slid along the sand. "Do you even know where you're going?"

    " 'Course not. But that's part of my charm," Agatha threw over her shoulder. She laughed to herself as he made face.

    Eternity grumbled to himself before he finally caught up to her wide strides. "You kicked me."

    "Yup. Sure did," she said with a bob of her head. "And you hit me with a chair." Agatha stuffed her hands into her pants pockets. "Oh, by the way. If you ever touch any of my family members, I'll spear your head through your ass."

    "You're a violent mortal," Eternity grumbled. "More violent than the early ones." "It's almost like someone fucked with my life until I had nothing left to lose," Agatha said with a laugh. She swayed with a hum. "So, Entropy...He sounds a lot like my kiddos. You leave those three unsupervised and suddenly you're in an alternate dimension fighting for your life." The goddess rolled her eyes with a forlorn grin before a thought occurred. "Hey, we're not stuck in here, are we?"

    Eternity growled deep in his chest. "You're the one who kicked us out of the astral plane. Shouldn't you know?"

    "Oh...right..." Agatha pursed her lips. "Well, I guess it's up to me to save the day for the hundredth time. At least I didn't die. That would have sucked."

    "I don't know if I like you," Eternity told her as he side-eyed her. "You're annoying." He stopped Agatha with a scowl. Eternity crossed his arms, flexing. (Ah! So, Loki liked muscly men. Agatha made sure to make a mental note for later.) "Why did you separate us from the others? Was it so you could try to kill me?"

    "You really are dense, aren't you?" Agatha removed her hands to try and open a portal back to her wife. Instead, she found Westview. "Well, that works too..." She waved for him to follow her. "I separated us because you couldn't stand to look at Death the way I couldn't all those years ago. You still need time, but you've taken the first step. So, there's that, at least." Eternity squinted as the sun blinded him. "Earth," he spat. "I detest this planet." "Agatha!" Wanda ran over with glowing hands. "Where did you go? Where's Rio?" Agatha gestured for the woman to stop talking. She was starting to think deities and cosmic entities didn't appreciate the lovely name she'd gifted her wife. And if that were the case, she definitely wasn't going to have others use it around the grouchy gods and the Universe. "We're on our way back to the astral plane. Just had to take a time-out to try and get things under control. Have you seen my Fulu coin? I think I lost it out here." She scanned the ground with a frown, a hand at her throat.

    The Scarlet Witch kept her eyes glued on the cosmic entity. "I haven't. The house and yard are..."

    The goddess looked at her property with a grimace. The whole place was absolutely trashed. And unfortunately, she couldn't blame anyone but herself for the mess. "Gonna need a couple of spells to throw everything back together," she joked dryly. Agatha shook her head, ignoring the hurt settling in her being. "No one's dead. That's what's important. Now, I just need to figure out how to get us back up into the astral plane for closing remarks..." Eternity huffed. He'd apparently been trying to open a portal while she was distracted. "You banished us, woman! We're stuck-"

    Agatha shoved him to the ground before tossing a bolt of purple lightning down at him as if she were Zeus. "What did I say? You know my name. Use it."

    Wanda gasped. "Can you do that? I'm pretty sure you're not supposed to do that." She looked between the two. "Oh...You've got a bit of blood...everywhere..."

    "Family reunion gone bad," Agatha grumbled. "Could you send us up?"

    "I don't think I can," Wanda whispered. "Oh! He's getting back up. What do we do?" "Eh, he's harmless. That's the twentieth time I've kicked his ass today." Agatha settled her hands on her hips. "Well...Did we learn our lesson?"

    Eternity growled as he lunged at her.

    "Taking that as a 'no'," she huffed before he sent them flying.

    They crashed into the rubble that had been her house. Wood broke and splintered underneath her as the cosmic entity wrapped his hands around her throat. Agatha grabbed at anything she could and repeatedly smashed bits against Eternity's head. He leaned back to clasp his hands together before bringing them down on her chest.

    There was a crunch and a hollow pop.

    "I think you broke my ribcage," Agatha huffed out in a whisper. "That's it...You're losing kneecap privileges." She gripped a shard of glass and imbued it with her magic. Agatha stabbed it into his left knee. She gave a breathy chuckle as he cried out. Wanda hoped onto Eternity's back and started to choke him with one of her superhero moves. He tried to grab at her, but the Scarlet Witch was a tiny thing compared to him. It only took a few more moments of the trio struggling with each other before Rio and Infinity descended to pull them apart.

    "You nearly decimated Australia," Loki hissed. "The realms are in a panic!" He tugged Eternity over to scold him. "For someone so ancient, you're acting like a child." Agatha lay back as she tried to figure out how the others popped their bones back into place. Rio rushed over and placed a hand against her chest. There was the tingle of fingers in her skin, and then Rio tugged. Her ribcage snapped and crunched like a dry penne noodle under a boot. Agatha exhaled loudly. She was going to need more than a hot bath after everything. "For the record," Agatha began once her voice had returned to normal, "we were having a lovely chat in a realm, dimension, whatever we were in. I only just pushed him because he refused to use my name. And, also for the record, I warned him I would kick his ass if he didn't."

    "You banished us," Eternity argued. "I live there! Have you no concept of shame?" "Well, maybe you need a change of scenery," she growled.

    "No more magic," Rio warned her. "You're too injured."

    Agatha ignored her and spread her hands, gesturing to the world around them. "I mean, look around you! There's so much everything! All of this, and people too. Entropy would have recycled into another person. Is probably still being recycled! Why would you risk missing him just because you feel..." Agatha dropped her arms back to her sides with a disbelieving laugh. "...Because you feel guilty?"

    Wanda gave her a reassuring smile.

    Eternity stared at her for a long time before taking a step back. "We're done here," he told Infinity. "Unbind me, so I can be on my way."

    "You shouldn't run from the truth," she told her brother. Her face softened as she removed whatever spell Agatha had cast. "It won't change anything, but reconnecting with your family will." Infinity rested a hand on his shoulder. "Brother...Don't you miss your sisters? Don't you miss, Death?"

    He looked between the two, face unreadable. Eternity took a step. Soon, he stood before Rio. He stared at her for a long time before he wrapped up in his arms. She froze, momentarily confused and shocked, before hugging her brother back just as tightly. There was the sound of sniffling as Eternity accepted his loss.

    Agatha threw herself back against the debris with a sigh. "...Did anybody grab Señor Scratchy and Mrs. Tejedora?"

    The Time Has Come to Go Chapter Summary A deal is made to secure their freedom. We say goodbye to an old friend. Chapter Notes Hehe!

    It's all about them '3's baby. Just one more chapter. Then, 3rd installment, here we come! 🤠 See the end of the chapter for more notes Her ugly house, the horrid thing she'd owned for seven years (almost eight now), was now a sad, sad, sad pile of rubble.

    Magic would fix her up. Would make sturdy walls anew. It would be as if nothing had happened, but Agatha would know. She would step through the front door and find fresh coats of paint and unblemished crown moulding. There would be no dents from the time Nicky swung a bat too hard and hit the edge of a doorway. Her kitchen would lack the tell- tale markings from when Rio first appeared in her home to threaten her with tears in her eyes. No bloodstained floors from their most harrowing moments.

    Agatha would sit on her bed, and the smell of lavender would be missing. Her pillows wouldn't have a trough where her head tended to rest once she'd drifted off to sleep. (Not that she would need to sleep anymore.) Her closet wouldn't be the mess it was when she last opened the door. The same would be said of her children's bedrooms. It would all look the same, but it wouldn't be home until they'd lived in it for several months. There was a terrible ache in her chest.

    So, the goddess sat in her backyard where her grave would have been and mourned her loss. Agatha lamented her home because her and that stupid, two-story house had seen and been through so much. It had been her friend before she ever understood the meaning of the word. It had kept her safe and warm when she needed it most. It was where her coven laid their heads. Where they dared to dream of a brighter future together as a family. Agatha shed a tear as she touched the empty space at her neck. Another casualty. She still hadn't found Alice's Fulu protection coin. It was a disheartening loss but Agatha would gladly lose if it meant her family was safe. She just wished it didn't sting as bad as it did. How would she break the news to her daughter?

    Rio stood with her siblings and Wanda as Agatha took time to herself. She fiddled with the hilt of her dagger, mouth twisted in a frown. Her wife was a sentimental witch. Once she connected with a person or a thing, she burrowed it deep in her soul. The destruction of her home had Agatha as low as she'd seen her since the loss of Alice. She didn't know how to help.

    There was movement to her left. "Are you sure you don't want to go to her?" Infinity asked quietly. "We won't stop you...Well, I won't." She shot their brother a look. Eternity rolled his eyes. "The w-" He scowled as his gaze flicked over to the goddess. "Agatha," he bit out, "is safe...for now. I can't guarantee anything if she kicks me again." Rio scoffed, equal parts irritated and playful. "Yeah, that's why you're making sure to say her name, right?"

    Her brother glared at her. It was a look that spoke of eons filled with hatred and distrust. "Had it not been for your wife, we wouldn't even be speaking." He lowered his gaze, suddenly remorseful. "But she is surprisingly insightful. I owe her for making the effort when we could have ended things in violence."

    Rio tilted her head as she contemplated his words. Whatever Agatha had said to him, it was enough to convince him to consider making amends. The power that her wife had! "I can only meet you halfway," she told him. "I cannot do the hardest parts for you. That is Life as much as it is Death."

    "That's beautiful," Wanda whispered. She blinked back tears before blowing her nose into a handkerchief she created from dust and pollen in the air. "Now go tell that to Agatha." Death shifted into her fleshy form and took a few tentative steps towards her wife. The goddess didn't twitch or show any signs of noticing her, which was a sign that she was truly lost in her emotions. Death wanted to claw her own heart out if it would make her feel better. She'd happily offer up her brother's pain should Agatha ask.

    "Go to her," Infinity urged. "Don't let us keep you apart another moment longer." She gently pushed Rio forward with a supportive nod of her head. "We'll stay right here." Rio looked back for a second before closing the gap between her and her goddess. "Agatha..." She fidgeted in place as she waited for blue eyes to land on her. Rio grew flowers out of habit. A tuft of purple and white dianthuses sprouted between her hands before she held them out.

    Agatha looked up at her and smiled wetly. "What kind are these? she asked. (Rio would never grow tired of that question.) She stood and accepted the bouquet readily. Agatha pressed them to her nose with a sniff.

    "They're dianthus flowers. 'Divine flowers' for the Divine Mother," Rio informed nervously. She found herself smiling back as Agatha took a hand to clasp in her own. "How generous of you," she teased, blue eyes twinkling. "I was just thinking about how we needed more flowers." Agatha took a step forward and pressed a chaste kiss to Rio's lips. "I hope I didn't get you into too much trouble. I wasn't trying to decimate the planet or anything."

    Rio grinned before bumping noses with her goddess. "No rules were broken, so I don't care. But it was so hot watching you beat the shit out of my brother," she breathed. Agatha laughed before pressing her face into Rio's neck. Rio hugged her tightly as the woman went back to grieving her house. Her flesh cooled as tears rolled down her shoulder and along her collarbone. Death absorbed the tears. Even if she were the earth and sky, the dirt didn't deserve to taste the salt of her wife's grief. She ran a finger up and down the goddess' spine, soothing her.

    "I'll be fine," Agatha told her after a long moment. She wiped away her tears and straightened her back. "Wanda will fix it et viola!" The goddess did her jazz hands before clearing her throat. "Come on. Let's see if 'Thing 1' and 'Thing 2' are gonna let us live happily ever after." Death stole a kiss before leading her wife back over to her siblings. "I believe we're ready to finish our conversation from earlier, Loki."

    The god visibly relaxed. "Thank the Allfather! Let's just finish here and be done with the whole thing."

    Infinity and Eternity sat themselves on randomly materializing furniture before Death followed suit. Wanda stood next to Agatha who was to the right of Rio. Loki removed his helmet and made his own chair. He leaned forward to address Eternity and Infinity. "I'm not sure how they're doing that," Death heard Agatha whisper to Wanda. She smiled inwardly at their confusion.

    "I am willing to recognize your union," Eternity began. "However, Agatha is not welcome in the astral plane. She leaves messes and is a rude god," he said with a scowl. "You won't get any complaints from me," Agatha told him. "Your house sucks, by the way. Not a splash of color."

    "And I already accept your marriage. All sixty-four of them," Infinity told her, ignoring Agatha's comments. "But I do agree with Eternity if only because you might want somewhere to sulk freely."

    Rio shared a look with Agatha who shrugged.

    "Would I need a reason to be over there?" her wife asked. "It's the lamest fucking place in existence."

    "Not necessarily. They could hold me hostage, though," Death reasoned playfully. Agatha scoffed. "As if they could keep me out if that happened. But I don't care for that bland, warehouse of a realm. I like your realm best." She grinned brightly, tear and bloodstained cheeks stretching. (Rio wanted to take a picture and have it etched into the side of a mountain or something.)

    "We're fine with this term," Death told her siblings. "Now, I'll leave you alone if you remove the Ancient Ones from power. We don't need them."

    "We don't kill," Infinity said, more towards Agatha. "But I'm certain we can retire the whole panel. What do you say, Eternity? Can you finally free Death from your oversight?" Her brother furrowed his brows. "How can I be certain you won't run around and fall in love with another mortal? Or try to create life with another mortal, too?"

    Rio rolled her eyes. "Perhaps you don't understand what Agatha and I did. I have halved myself, as did she. I will never love another except for my wife. And, as it happens, Agatha is no longer a mortal, but a certified god. Does that answer satisfy your concerns?" "And what about your duty? Will you continue to carry it out?" Eternity leaned forward with a sniff. "You won't get distracted?"

    Agatha leaned forward, a hand on Death's shoulder. "If anything, I'll be an incentive," she purred. "You'd work twice as hard if I asked you to, right my love?" She flashed her wife a wolfish grin that was anything but innocent.

    Rio swallowed. "Agatha can be persuasive," she told her siblings. "Very persuasive." Infinity gave her a knowing look. "I'm aware." She turned her head to face their brother. "Eternity, I can vouch for our dear sister. She's more well-behaved when she doesn't have your sword resting at her throat. The only times she's bent the rules were to balance out your cruelties."

    "Of which there were many," Loki muttered with an eye roll.

    Eternity shot the god a look. "I thought you were my friend," he grumbled. "I'm a friend to all," Loki reminded. He tugged at the lapels to his coat as he gave Eternity a crooked grin. "But a true friend is willing to help you see when you've been wrong. Lady Death has only recently retaliated against you and your Ancient Ones. Which, might I remind you, you set forth before the first mortal drew breath. So, in the grand scheme of things, Lady Death has endured your punishment far longer than necessary. She has willingly obeyed your every law until four hundred years ago, which is nothing compared to all of your long, long existences."

    Death nodded her thanks. "I'm not asking to be free of my duties, Eternity. I just want those harpies to leave Agatha and I alone." She bobbed her head from side to side. "And to let us work some of our frustration out on them."

    Eternity scowled. "I will remove them from their stations, but we will not destroy their souls." He sent a pointed look in Agatha's direction.

    The goddess rolled her eyes.

    Infinity hummed before crossing her legs in the opposite direction. "Brother," she soothed. "You can do better than that. How are you to show you're willing to change if you won't put forth the effort? This is our darling sister, after all."

    Death eyed Infinity. They had spoken over the years, yes, but she had never felt supported. Then again, Infinity had never blamed her for Entropy's death. She had only faulted Death for reaping his soul and causing Eternity anguish. It had to be a sibling thing after watching Billy blame Wanda and Agatha for Tommy. Once things had settled, if they were settled, Death would have a talk with her sister about her sudden change of heart.

    "What more would you have me do? I have just freed her of my oversight." Her brother rubbed a temple before sighing so heavily the ground shook. "Death," Eternity said gravely, "would you employ the five of them to gather souls for you? Doing so would let you have more free time for your," his eyes landed on Agatha, "wife."

    The couple practically leapt at the offer.

    "I think I can make that work for us," Death said, salivating. "I'll position them around the rest of the cosmos. That'll make this next part much easier."

    "What part?" Infinity questioned with a quirked brow.

    "Mandatory family dinners. You should get to know your nephews and niece," Agatha told the cosmic entities with a flourish of her coat. "Alice has a way with hugs, and the boys are sure to entertain you. Trust me. The sooner you give in to them, the easier it is to go about your day."

    "You think I want to spend time with mortals?" Eternity laughed. "I have more important things to tend to."

    "Oh, please," Agatha scoffed. "If you did, you wouldn't still be here. Plus, you're a couch potato. You need the exercise." She flapped a wrist gayly towards Eternity. "Now, what day would you like to stop by? We'll start them next week once the house has been sorted back into place." The goddess cast a forlorn glance at the pile of wood and glass. Infinity shifted. "We appreciate the offer, but..."

    Rio grinned inwardly as she used her profound experience to turn the tables. "Sorry, Agatha. I guess they don't intend to get to know the family. But in our solitude, we'll have plenty of time to explain to Nicky why his aunt and uncle don't want to see him. Maybe in a few millennia, they will. Ah...he'll be dead by that point, so I guess he won't." Her wife, the quick study that she was, played her part perfectly. Agatha crossed her arms with a huff. "It wasn't enough stealing him away from us at such a young age, now they want to ignore his existence, too? Maybe I don't want our children around them. I couldn't dare to break their hearts. What do you think, Wanda?"

    The Scarlet Witch shook her head, wiping a tear away. She blew her nose again, loudly. "I thought you'd done well to show them otherwise. I'll break the news to them. There's no need to make Nicky so upset with you after everything." Wanda wrung her hands. "Not to mention, they're the reason why your house is in pieces." She weaved her magic around the decimated debris and worked to slowly piece it back together.

    Infinity laughed; a hand pressed to her lips. "You make an interesting argument, Your Brilliance. I will attend your family dinners."

    "You can't be serious?" Eternity sighed. He let his shoulders sag as the women and Loki fixed him with a look. "Twice a month. No more than that," he reasoned.

    "We can do that," Agatha said as she bumped Rio with an elbow. "Was that everything, my love?"

    "My realm," Death all but begged. "Return it to me." Her being ached as her full form reached towards the space blocked from her. She circled it like a black cloud. (Always circling. Always waiting.)

    Eternity clapped his hands together before slowly separating them. With it, Death felt Frida's barrier shake and tremble and melt away. She gripped at her seat as power and feeling and life and death and rebirth settled back deep into her being. The entirety of the cosmos was now at her fingertips as it always should have been.

    Simultaneously, Agatha yelped sharply. "A heads-up would have been nice," she growled. The goddess pressed at her chest with a scowl thrown in the trio's direction. "Felt like I got stabbed again. Fuck!"

    Death stood with a laugh as she inhaled deeply. "I am whole," she sighed happily. Rio turned to her wife and pulled her in for a deep, deep kiss.

    The connecting of their lips was like the implosion of a supernova. Like the crash of a wave against a rocky shore. Like the unfurling of a butterfly's wings for their first flight. Like the kiss of snow upon the earth after a long summer. Like stepping into Agatha's house and seeing her wife's face light up with all the love in her heart.

    "Oh," Agatha laughed breathlessly. "I wanna do that again."

    So they did.

    "Okay you two," Wanda said, clearing her throat. "Let's, uh...Let's take a break before things get anymore hectic around here."

    Rio broke away from Agatha's grasp to look around her. The grass and a few saplings had sprouted around them. That would be fine if it weren't for the fact that they were still in the midst of winter and the ground was churned like a field for planting. The cosmic entity huffed a laugh with a proud smirk. Leave it to Agatha to make her lose control of her magic. "If that's everything settled, I think I'll get back to my duties," Loki told the group. "You've a lovely home, Agatha. I'll stop by later for some more cookies, Wanda. My father was exceedingly impressed with them." He gave the group of higher beings a bow before zipping away in a shower of green glitter.

She turned to Agatha and clasped a hand in both of hers. "Congratulations, my dear. I'm happy for the both of you." She patted the hand before stepping back. Eternity kept his distance from the Divine Mother. He side-eyed her with a, "Thank you for your insight, Agatha. I hope to gain your clarity someday."

    "Hey, don't rush it," Agatha told him. She punched him lightly on the shoulder. "Just don't take it out on my wife or there will be consequences," she said with a deadly sweet tone. "I'd let you meet the kiddos, but they've had enough excitement this week. I think we'd rather just relax for once."

    Rio stepped forward to take her brother's hand. "I'm here for you, Eternity." He nodded in her direction before slipping his hand from hers. "I must be off." Eternity dissolved into sand without another word.

    "I wouldn't worry about him," Agatha said. "I've got a feeling he'll be around more." She settled herself behind her wife. Her arms wrapped around Rio's middle as her chin rested on a shoulder. "You, my love, should see to your new employees while I tend to our children." "I could help," she reasoned. "We can deliver the good news together." Agatha shook her head. "Go," she whispered, "so we can bless your palace...Alone." Death's eyes widened as she swallowed thickly. "I'll be home as quickly as possible," she vowed.

    "Then hop to it," Agatha instructed. She pressed a kiss to her wife's cheek before pushing her forward. "And I expect results, my love."

    Infinity grinned knowingly. "Best to listen to your wife, Death."

    Rio turned in Agatha's arms for another kiss that sent a thrill down her spine. "Te veo."

    Agatha grinned smugly as Rio slipped away into the Ether with the Ancient Ones' souls. Agatha quirked a brow as Infinity remained. "Did you...need something?" The woman with her golden toga and flawless hair stepped forward one last time. "I just wanted to reassure you that your feelings for Death are your own. All I did was send was your soul out into the world. Everything that came to be was your own doing, Agatha." The goddess pursed her lips as she twirled the end of a strand of hair. "So, I wasn't some pawn in a sick game to bring Death to her knees?" Because if she was, Agatha had Death on her knees for entirely different, sinful reasons.

    Infinity shook her head. Her eyes twinkled as if she'd heard Agatha's thoughts. "I simply wanted to give my sister a reprieve from her loneliness. But again, it was all up to chance. The only person who tethered you to Death was Evanora, and even she could not have divined the depth of your devotion." Infinity ghosted a hand along the side of Agatha's head and down her shoulder. "The witch who chased Death. Death, who chased Life. Two halves, united at last. The Sacred Balance restored."

    Agatha released the strand of hair to twist and swish her coat. "Well...that is a relief. Thanks." The goddess wasn't sure if she was supposed to bow like Loki had, but her body had already started the process. She gave a curt, half-assed bow and grimaced. "Y'know, I'm just gonna..." She pointed behind her, lips pursing. "Good...good seeing you. Come back...whenever." Good job, Harkness, she told herself with a mental kick. We were badass until you had to lose your nerve.

    She felt the grass (that was new) flatten and spring back as the last cosmic entity disappeared. Agatha turned back to Wanda. The witch gave her a 'what the hell just happened' look. Agatha shrugged. "Don't ask me. I'm just as lost as you are." She nudged a fork with her foot. "Did Billy get back to you?"

    Wanda nodded. "Seems they'd snuck into Nicky's bag instead of waiting in the basement. Señor Scratchy and Mrs. Tejedora are safe and sound. Although Teddy did have a panic attack when Mrs. Tejedora snuck into his room. Other than that, Eastview was spared the nightmare weather."

    "Nightmare weather?" Agatha asked, brows furrowed.

    "Oh...I guess you wouldn't know unless you were here. Everything sort of dissolved into chaos." Wanda grimaced as the first floor finished piecing itself back together. "Everybody was freaking out. It took a lot to convince them that I hadn't blown all of you up in a fit of rage."

    "Whoops...Did they give you any trouble?"

    Wanda shook her head. "I let the police do a quick scan for bodies. They left once I did a bit of mind control. They almost found the Ancient Ones."

    Agatha smirked. "My quick thinking is rubbing off on you," she said, preening. The goddess pressed at her shoulder with a hiss. She didn't know what hurt more. Her body, or the loss of her home. "If you fix the house, I'll get the kids," Agatha proposed. "Because if I'm being honest, I can't look at that any longer or I'm going to kick somebody's ass again." Wanda laughed. "I want to hear all about the family reunion when you get back. Also, you need a healing bath soon because you're very bruised. How did you even get most of that?" "Eternity likes to play rough, but he's not as endearing as Rio." Agatha let go of her arm and wiped the blood off on her pants. "Oh, you wouldn't believe the drama of it all. But that's for later. Be back in a jiffy!"

    The return of Rio's realm meant some added bonuses for Agatha. The world was bursting with colors mortal humans couldn't possibly dream of seeing. And the wind carried scents and stories from the places they'd been. It was like she was a part of the sky and the ground and the heavens and separate all at once. Actually, it made her a bit dizzy the more she thought about it. That pressure from earlier had finally left, but not the way the world seemed to wobble from time to time. (Agatha decided to ask Rio about it later.) But they'd won! Ancient Ones gone. Eternity placated. Infinity...well, Infinity hadn't actually done anything but hold Rio back. Agatha decided she would be mad about that later. She was just relieved they were finally free. Death was free and whole at last. Agatha loved her wife so much that she would willingly fight the Universe a second time if need be. The look of relief and joy that had washed over Rio's face had made every little inconvenience worth it. The smile that had blossomed on those lush lips had nearly forced Agatha to her knees. And that kiss!

    Herself! What a kiss!

    Agatha practically swooned at the thought as she dove towards Teddy's house. It was a sizeable abode made of brick with a winding driveway. Billy's Subaru was settled on the front lawn. The goddess landed on the front porch as if she weighed nothing before knocking on the front door. A man about Jeff's age, which was somewhere between thirty and eighty, appeared. Agatha plastered a smile on her face having completely forgotten that she was bloody, battered, and bruised.

    "Hiya there! I'm here to pick up my family. Should be one man, three women, two teens, and a small child. Oh! And to see Teddy, of course," Agatha said in greeting. "I'm Agatha Harkness, Divine Mother, Triple Goddess, the Life part of Life and Death. Pleasure to meet you!"

    The man stared at her for a long moment. "Uh.......Eddie?"

    "Dad?" Teddy appeared before beaming at the witch. "Agatha!" He stepped out to throw his arms around her. Teddy eyed her with a pleased look. "You're glowing! Bloody, but glowing." "Oh, you," she said, waving him off with a blush. "This is why you're my favorite," Agatha whispered. "How've you been, kiddo? Did you get taller?"

    "Ha! I wish. Come in, come in. Dad, this is Billy's private tutor and mother." Teddy finished introducing the two with a wide grin. "Seriously, though! You look amazing.
